\displaystyle F(s_{*})>\frac{\alpha-n(\gamma-1)}{\alpha}\Big(\frac{\alpha B_{n,\gamma,\alpha}}{n(\gamma-1)}\Big)^{\frac{n(\gamma-1)}{n(\gamma-1)-\alpha}}M^{\varepsilon,b,\alpha}_{\rm c}(\gamma)^{\frac{\gamma(2n-\alpha)-2n}{n(\gamma-1)-\alpha}}=E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},

(3.16)

and

\displaystyle s_{*}=\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-n(\gamma-1)}F(s_{*})>\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-n(\gamma-1)}E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}>2E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}.

(3.17)

Now, using (
3.13
) and (
3.15
)–(
3.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle F(\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)\leq E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}<F(s_{*}),

(3.18)

\displaystyle\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}e(\rho_{0})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}<s_{*}.

(3.19)

Thus, since
t\mapsto\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r
is continuous with respect to
t
,
we must have

\displaystyle\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<s_{*},

as otherwise we can apply the intermediate value theorem to derive a contradiction to (
3.18
).
This implies

\displaystyle F(\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-B_{n,\gamma,\alpha}M^{\frac{\gamma(2n-\alpha)-2n}{n(\gamma-1)}}s_{*}^{\frac{\alpha-n(\gamma-1)}{n(\gamma-1)}}\Big)\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha-n(\gamma-1)}{\alpha}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

(3.20)

Therefore, using (
3.13
), (
3.15
), and (
3.20
), we derive (
3.6
).

5. For Case 4, we first consider
\alpha=0
.
For
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\geq 1
,
we know that
\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\geq\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\geq 0
.
Then, if
|\boldsymbol{y}|\geq|\boldsymbol{x}|
,

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)=\frac{1}{2}\log((|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}\log(4+4|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})=\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}).

If
|\boldsymbol{x}|\geq|\boldsymbol{y}|
, then

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\leq\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}).

Thus, we obtain that, for
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\geq 1
,

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\leq\log 4+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}).

(3.21)

Then, by (
3.21
) and the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho(\Phi_{0}*\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\boldsymbol{x})\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\boldsymbol{y})\Big(\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y})}\rho(\boldsymbol{x})\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{-1}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y})^{c}}\rho(\boldsymbol{x})\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\boldsymbol{y})\bigg(\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y}))}\|\log(|\cdot|)\|_{L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(B_{1})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,+\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\geq 1}\rho(\boldsymbol{x})\Big(\log 4+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\bigg){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\Big(1+\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(\Omega_{t})}+\int_{\Omega_{t}}\rho(\boldsymbol{x})\log(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+C(M)\Big(1+\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(r)\log(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\Big),

(3.22)

where we have applied the Young inequality in the final line.

6. For
\alpha\in(-1,0)
, we see that, for
\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

|\Phi_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})|\leq\frac{2^{-\alpha}}{-\alpha}\big(k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\big).

(3.23)

Thus, employing a similar method to (
3.22
), we use (
3.23
) to obtain

\left|\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\right|\leq\frac{2^{1-\alpha}M}{-\alpha}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

(3.24)

Considering the latter integral in (
3.22
) and (
3.24
),
but in generality for
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, from (
2.10
) and integration by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\left(\rho uk_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r\right)(t,b(t))+\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho_{t}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\left(\rho uk_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r\right)(t,b(t))-\int^{b(t)}_{a}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})(\rho ur^{n-1})_{r}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\int^{b(t)}_{a}(k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2}))_{r}\rho ur^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho ur^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho(u^{2}+1)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho u^{2}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{M}{2\omega_{n}}.

(3.25)

Integrating (
3.25
) in
t
, we see that, for
\alpha\in(-1,0]
,

\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{Mt}{2\omega_{n}}+\frac{1}{2}\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho u^{2}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s.

(3.26)

Hence, combining (
3.22
), (
3.24
), and (
3.26
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\bigg|+\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho e(\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+C(M,T)\Big(1+\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho u^{2}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\Big).

(3.27)

Combining (
3.13
) and (
3.27
), we obtain that, for
\alpha\in(-1,0]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho\big(u^{2}+e(\rho)+k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho\big(u_{r}^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad\,+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)^{n-2}{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{2}u_{0}^{2}+e(\rho_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\quad\,+C(M,T)\bigg(1+\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho u^{2}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\bigg).

(3.28)

Applying the Grönwall inequality on (
3.28
) and then using (
B.10
), we conclude

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho\big(u^{2}+e(\rho)+k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho\big(u_{r}^{2}+\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad\,+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)^{n-2}{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(M,T)\bigg(1+\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{2}u_{0}^{2}+e(\rho_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})+k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},T).

∎

Remark 3.5
.

Adding
\frac{\kappa M^{2}}{2\alpha}=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho(\frac{1}{\alpha}*\rho)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=\frac{\kappa}{2}\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}(\frac{1}{\alpha}*\rho_{0})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r
to both sides of (
3.5
)
yields

\displaystyle\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+e(\rho)+\frac{\kappa}{2}\big((\frac{1}{\alpha}+\Phi_{\alpha}\big)*\rho\big)\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho\Big(u_{r}^{2}+(n-1)\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad+(n-1)\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)^{n-2}{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{2}u_{0}^{2}+e(\rho_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\big((\frac{1}{\alpha}+\Phi_{\alpha})*\rho_{0}\big)\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

(3.29)

We can formally see that, using (
1.2
), as
\alpha\to 0
,
(
3.29
) converges to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{2}u^{2}+e(\rho)+\frac{\kappa}{2}(\Phi_{0}*\rho)\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho\Big(u_{r}^{2}+(n-1)\frac{u^{2}}{r^{2}}\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad\,+(n-1)\varepsilon\int^{t}_{0}(\rho u^{2})(s,b(s))b(s)^{n-2}{\,\rm d}s\\
&=\int^{b}_{a}\rho_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{2}u_{0}^{2}+e(\rho_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}(\Phi_{0}*\rho_{0})\Big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.\end{split}

Corollary 3.6

(Uniform Estimates)
.

Under the conditions of
Lemma
3.4
, the following uniform estimates in
(\varepsilon,b)

(
for
\varepsilon
small enough and
b
large enough
)
hold
:

Case
1:

\kappa=-1
with
\gamma>1
for
\alpha\in(0,n-2]
or
\gamma>\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}
for
\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho\big(1+u^{2}+\rho^{\gamma-1}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C(M,E_{0});

(3.30)

Case
2:

\alpha\in(0,n-1)
and
\kappa=1
with
\gamma>\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho\big(1+u^{2}+\rho^{\gamma-1}\big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C(M,E_{0});

(3.31)

Case
3:

\alpha\in(-1,0]
and
\kappa\in\{-1,1\}
with
\gamma>1
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho\big(1+u^{2}+\rho^{\gamma-1}+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})\big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C(M,E_{0},T),

(3.32)

where we have used the convention that
\rho
and
u
are zero outside of
\Omega_{t}
.
In particular, the following uniform estimates in
(\varepsilon,b)

(
for
\varepsilon
small enough and
b
large enough
)
hold
:
For
Cases 1–2
,

\displaystyle\|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|(\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0});

and for
Case 3
, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, there exists
C(M,E_{0},T,K)>0
such that

\displaystyle\|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\|(\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\leq C(M,E_{0},T,K).

Proof.

Estimates (
3.30
)–(
3.32
) follow from Lemma
3.4

and the fact that
\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}
,
e(\rho)=\frac{a_{0}}{\gamma-1}\rho^{\gamma-1}
,
and

E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\to E_{0}
as
b\to\infty
and
\varepsilon\to 0
due to Lemma
B.8
.

For the second part of the corollary, for Case 2 or
\kappa=-1
and
\gamma>\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}
, by the HLS inequality
in Lemma
A.2
and interpolation, we have

\displaystyle\|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\frac{C_{n,\alpha}}{\alpha}\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma(2n-\alpha)-2n}{n(\gamma-1)}}\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\alpha\gamma}{n(\gamma-1)}}\leq C(M,E_{0}),

\displaystyle\|(\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\frac{C_{n,\alpha+1}}{\alpha}\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma(2n-\alpha)-2n}{n(\gamma-1)}}\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\alpha\gamma}{n(\gamma-1)}}\leq C(M,E_{0}),

where the final inequalities follow from the first part of this corollary.

For Case 1, we use the Riesz composition formula in Theorem
A.5
, the HLS inequality in Lemma
A.2
,
and the Tonelli theorem to obtain

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha}\big\||\cdot|^{-\alpha}*\rho\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2},\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}}\Big\||\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\big)\Big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n,\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}}{\alpha\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2},\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}}\Big\||\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{n,\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}}{\alpha\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2},\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho\,\Big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\big)\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-C_{n,\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0}),

and, for
\alpha\in(0,n-2)
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\big\||\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}*\rho\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2}-1,\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}}\Big\||\cdot|^{-\left(1+\frac{n+\alpha}{2}\right)}*\big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\big)\Big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{n,\frac{n+\alpha}{2}+1}}{\alpha\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2}-1,\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}}\Big\||\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(3.33)

where
\kappa_{\alpha,\beta}
is defined in (
A.4
).

For
\alpha=n-2
, it follows directly
that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\||\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(3.34)

Now, applying Theorem
A.5
and the Tonelli theorem, we have

\displaystyle\Big\||\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho\,\Big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\big(|\cdot|^{-\frac{n+\alpha}{2}}*\rho\big)\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{\frac{n-\alpha}{2},\frac{n-\alpha}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0}).

(3.35)

Tying together (
3.33
)–(
3.35
),
we obtain that, for
\kappa=-1
and
\alpha\in(0,n-2]
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0}).

For
\alpha=0
, for simplicity, we may assume that
K=\overline{B_{D}(\mathbf{0})}
for some
D>0
, where
B_{D}(\mathbf{0})
denotes the ball with origin at
\mathbf{0}
and radius
D
.
Notice that, for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
and
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with

|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\geq 1
,
\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\geq\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\geq 0
.
Then, if
\boldsymbol{y}\in K
,

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\leq\log(1+2D).

If
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus K
, then

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)=\frac{1}{2}\log((|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)^{2})\leq\frac{1}{2}\log(4+4|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})=\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}).

Thus, for
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

\log(|\boldsymbol{x}|+|\boldsymbol{y}|)\leq\log(1+2D)+\log 2+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})=\log(2+4D)+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}).

(3.36)

Then, for
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
, we apply the Hölder inequality, (
3.36
),
and (
3.7
) to obtain

\displaystyle|(\Phi_{0}*\rho)(\boldsymbol{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x})}\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{-1})\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}+\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}\log(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq\|\log(|\cdot|)\|_{L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(B_{1})}\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x}))}+\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}\big(\log(2+4D)+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\big)\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x}))}+\log(2+4D)\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c})}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}\log(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

(3.37)

Therefore, we have

\|\Phi_{0}*\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

For
\alpha\in(-1,0)
, we assume again that
K=\overline{B_{D}}
for some
D>0
.
Using (
3.23
), we obtain that, for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

|\Phi_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})|\leq\frac{2^{-\alpha}}{-\alpha}\left(k_{\alpha}(1+D^{2})+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\right).

(3.38)

Thus, similar to (
3.37
), using (
3.38
) and (
3.7
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(\boldsymbol{x})|&\leq\frac{2^{-\alpha}}{-\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(k_{\alpha}(1+D^{2})+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\right)\rho(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).\end{split}

Then we obtain

\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

For
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, take
1<p<\min\{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2},\gamma\}
.
Applying the HLS inequality in Lemma
A.2
, interpolation, and (
3.7
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq\|(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}\mathds{1}_{B_{1}})*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}+\|(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}\mathds{1}_{B_{1}^{c}})*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq\|(|\cdot|^{-(1+\frac{\alpha}{2}+n(1-\frac{1}{p}))})*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}+\|\mathds{1}_{B_{1}^{c}}*\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n,1+\frac{\alpha}{2}+n(1-\frac{1}{p})}\|\rho\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|K|^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2n}}\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n,1+\frac{\alpha}{2}+n(1-\frac{1}{p})}\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma-p}{p(\gamma-1)}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma(p-1)}{p(\gamma-1)}}+|K|^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2n}}\|\rho\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T).

∎

Remark 3.7
.

It is still open for the repulsive case
\kappa=-1
and
\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)

without any restriction on
\gamma
,
owing to the fact that the
L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}
–norm
of the derivative of the potential term cannot be bounded by the potential energy of the system.

3.3.
Expanding the domain

We need to ensure that, as
b\to\infty
, our domain
\Omega^{T}
converges to the whole space

\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Lemma 3.8

(Expanding of domain
\Omega^{T}
)
.

Given
T>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, there exists a positive constant
C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon)>0
such that,
if
b\geq\max\{C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon),\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\}

(
as defined in
Lemma
B.8
)
,

\displaystyle b(t)\geq\frac{b}{2}\qquad\,\,\mbox{for $t\in[0,T]$}.

(3.39)

This theorem can be achieved by following the same argument as in
[
26
, Lemma 3.4]
.
Therefore, as
b\to\infty
,
\lim_{b\to\infty}\inf_{t\in[0,T]}b(t)=\infty
, as we desire.

3.4.
BD-type entropy

We now need to make uniform estimates on the derivatives of the density in order
to apply the Aubin-Lions lemma to prove the convergence
of
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})
as
b\to\infty

to global weak solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
of (
1.5
).
We do so by proving a BD entropy bound, taking advantage of the fact that
our viscosity terms
satisfy
\lambda(\rho)=\rho\mu^{\prime}(\rho)-\mu(\rho)
.
We have two different BD-type estimates: The first is to follow the same approach
as in
[
26
]
for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
due to the inability to
integrate by parts rigorously for
\alpha>n-2
,
as
\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho
is only twice differentiable for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
.
This approach fails to provide a suitable estimate in the attractive case
\kappa=1
, due to the lack of the control of the boundary term
a^{n-1}(\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})(t,a)

as we have no
a priori
of the density at the lower boundary
r=a
except for (
2.15
).
Moreover, this is insufficient, as we require this to be a uniform bound in
b
,
which is not the case.
Although, for
\alpha=n-2
, we have no issue, as we can show that the boundary term disappears.
However, in the repulsive case
\kappa=-1
, we can show that the boundary term has favourable sign
and can thus be controlled.

Lemma 3.9

(BD-Type Entropy Estimate I)
.

Under the conditions of
Lemma
3.4
,
for
\kappa=-1
with
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
or
\kappa=1
with
\alpha=n-2
, any given
T>0
,

\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, and
b\geq\max\{C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon),\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\}

(
as defined in
Lemmas
3.8
and
B.8
)
, the following estimate holds
:

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big|(\sqrt{\rho(t,r)})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{4a_{0}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}|(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})_{r}|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(t,b(t)))b(t)^{n}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}p(\rho(s,b(s)))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,b(s)))b(s)^{n}{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq C(E_{0},M,T)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1. We first consider (
2.10
) in Lagrangian coordinates.
Taking the derivative of the mass equation
\eqref{lag}_{1}
with respect to
x
, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{x\tau}=-\big(\rho^{2}(r^{n-1}u)_{x}\big)_{x}.

Plugging it into
\eqref{lag}_{2}
leads to

\displaystyle u_{\tau}+r^{n-1}p_{x}=-\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x\tau}-\varepsilon(n-1)r^{n-2}\rho_{x}u-\kappa\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}.

(3.40)

Furthermore, (
3.40
) can be simplified to

\displaystyle(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})_{\tau}+r^{n-1}p_{x}=-\kappa\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}.

(3.41)

Now, multiplying (
3.41
) by
u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x}
and integrating with respect to
x

over
[0,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}r^{2n-2}p(\rho)_{x}\rho_{x}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}r^{n-1}p(\rho)_{x}u{\,\rm d}x

\displaystyle=-\kappa\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}u\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}{\,\rm d}x-\kappa\varepsilon\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\rho\rho_{x}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}{\,\rm d}x.

(3.42)

Using (
3.11
), we can rewrite (
3.42
) in the form:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}r^{2n-2}p(\rho)_{x}\rho_{x}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}r^{n-1}p(\rho)_{x}u{\,\rm d}x

\displaystyle=-\frac{\kappa}{2}\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho){\,\rm d}x-\kappa\varepsilon\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\rho\rho_{x}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}{\,\rm d}x.

(3.43)

Using
\eqref{lag}_{1}
and (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\tau}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})=-(\rho^{2}(r^{n-1}u)_{x})(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})=-\frac{a_{0}\rho^{\gamma}}{\varepsilon}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}).

(3.44)

Now, applying
\eqref{lag}_{1}
, (
2.18
), and (
3.44
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p(\rho)_{x}u\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}x

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p(\rho)(r^{n-1}u)_{x}{\,\rm d}x+(p(\rho)ur^{n-1})(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho){\,\rm d}x+(p(\rho)r^{n-1})(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})\,r_{\tau}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho){\,\rm d}x+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}-\frac{1}{n}p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))\rho_{\tau}(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}})b(\tau)^{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho){\,\rm d}x+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}.

(3.45)

Substituting (
3.45
) into (
3.43
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\Big\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}\,{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho{\,\rm d}x\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\varepsilon\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\rho\rho_{x}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}{\,\rm d}x

\displaystyle=-\kappa\varepsilon\left[\rho^{2}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}\right]^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}+\kappa\varepsilon\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\rho(\rho r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x})_{x}{\,\rm d}x.

(3.46)

2. Consider the last integral of (
3.46
). Using the HLS inequality in Lemma
A.2
,
we obtain the estimate for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\rho\big(\rho r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}\big)_{x}{\,\rm d}x\\
&=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{\Omega_{t}}(\rho\Delta(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho))(t,\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-(\alpha+2)}}(\Omega_{t})}\|\Delta(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\
&=\frac{\alpha+1}{\omega_{n}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-(\alpha+2)}}(\Omega_{t})}\big\|\,|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+2)}*\rho\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\
&\leq\frac{C_{\alpha+2,n}(\alpha+1)}{\omega_{n}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-(\alpha+2)}}(\Omega_{t})}^{2}.\end{split}

Case 1:
\kappa=-1
.
We can show that the last term on the RHS of (
3.46
) is negative as

\Delta\Phi_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}\frac{(n-2)-\alpha}{|\,\cdot\,|^{\alpha+2}}\quad&\mbox{for $\alpha\in(-1,n-2)$},\\[5.69054pt]
\omega_{n}\delta_{0}&\mbox{for $\alpha=n-2$},\end{cases}

in the sense of distributions, where
\delta_{0}
is the Dirac mass centred at 0.
Then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho(\rho r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x})_{x}=\Delta(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)&=\begin{cases}(n-2-\alpha)(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+2)}*\rho)&\mbox{for $\alpha\in(-1,n-2)$},\\
\omega_{n}\rho&\mbox{for $\alpha=n-2$}\end{cases}\\[5.69054pt]
&\geq 0.\end{split}

(3.47)

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\Big\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}\,{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho{\,\rm d}x\Big\}\\
&\quad+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\\
&\leq-\kappa\varepsilon\left[\rho^{2}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}\right]^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}.\end{split}

Case 2:
\kappa=1
and
\gamma\geq\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha-2}
.
Then we can control the integral by the internal energy:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha-2)}}(\Omega_{t})}^{2}\leq C\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho\big(1+e(\rho)\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.\end{split}

Case 3:
\kappa=1
and
\gamma\in(\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha},\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha-2})
.
Applying the interpolation yields

\displaystyle\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha-2}}(\Omega_{t})}=\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{n\gamma}{n-2}}(\Omega_{t})}^{\theta}\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(\Omega_{t})}^{1-\theta}\qquad\text{ with }\theta=\frac{2n+(\alpha+2-2n)\gamma}{4}.

This can be controlled as in
[
26
]
.

3. Now, looking at the second to the last term
of (
3.46
), we have

\big[\rho^{2}r^{2(n-1)}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}\big]^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}=\big[\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}\big]^{b(t)}_{a}.

From (
3.47
), we see that
r\mapsto r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}
is increasing in
r
.
Since
\lim_{r\searrow 0}r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}=0
,
then
a^{n-1}(\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})(t,a)\geq 0
, owing to the fact that
\rho(t,a)>0
.

In the case that
\kappa=-1
, we can ignore this term as it has a negative sign.

In the case that
\alpha=n-2
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}(r^{n-1}(\Phi_{n-2}*\rho)_{r})_{r}=r^{n-1}\Delta(\Phi_{n-2}*\rho)=\omega_{n}r^{n-1}\rho(t,r)=0\qquad\mbox{for all $r\in(0,a)$}.\end{split}

Hence,
r\mapsto r^{n-1}(\Phi_{n-2}*\rho)_{r}
is constant in
r\in(0,a)
.
Since
\lim_{r\searrow 0}r^{n-1}(\Phi_{n-2}*\rho)_{r}=0
, then

a^{n-1}(\rho(\Phi_{n-2}*\rho)_{r})(t,a)=0
.

4. We now focus on the other boundary term for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2)
to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\frac{b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}}{|b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}|^{\alpha+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{e_{1}}{\,\rm d}\sigma(\boldsymbol{y})\bigg|\leq\omega_{n}|b(s)-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)}.

(3.48)

Employing (
3.48
), (
3.39
), (
3.1
), and
a=\frac{1}{b}
,
we obtain that, for
\eta\in(a,b(s))
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\frac{b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}}{|b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}|^{\alpha+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{e_{1}}{\,\rm d}\sigma(\boldsymbol{y})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\{|b(s)-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)},\,(\eta b(s))^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}\}

\displaystyle\leq C\min\{|b(s)-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)},\,1\}\leq C.

(3.49)

From Lemma 3.4 in
[
26
]
, we see that, for
b\geq\max\{C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon),\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{t}_{0}u(s,b(s)){\,\rm d}s\bigg|\leq\frac{1}{4}b.

(3.50)

Using (
2.16
), (
3.49
), and (
3.50
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bigg|\int^{t}_{0}(\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})(s,b(s)){\,\rm d}s\bigg|\\
&\leq\int^{t}_{0}\rho_{0}(b)\Big(\int^{b(s)}_{a}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\frac{b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}}{|b(s)\boldsymbol{e_{1}}-\eta\boldsymbol{y}|^{\alpha+2}}\cdot\boldsymbol{e_{1}}{\,\rm d}\sigma(\boldsymbol{y})\Big|\rho(s,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big)b(s)^{n-1}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C\int^{t}_{0}\rho_{0}(b)\Big(\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho(s,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big)b(s)^{n-1}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C(M)\int^{t}_{0}\rho_{0}(b)b(s)^{n-1}{\,\rm d}s\\
&=C(M)\int^{t}_{0}\rho_{0}(b)\Big(b+\int^{s}_{0}u(\tau,b(\tau)){\,\rm d}\tau\Big)^{n-1}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C(M)\int^{t}_{0}\rho_{0}(b)b^{n-1}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C(M,T)b^{-(n-\beta)}b^{n-1}\\
&\leq C(M,T),\end{split}

where we have used that
\rho_{0}(b)\cong b^{-(n-\beta)}
, with
\beta=\min\{\frac{1}{2},(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})n\}
. We can also obtain this bound for
\alpha=n-2
from
[
26
]
. Hence, similar to
[
26
]
, we see that,
for
\kappa=-1
with
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
or
\kappa=1
with
\alpha=n-2
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big|(\sqrt{\rho(t,r)})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{4a_{0}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\big|(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(t,b(t)))b(t)^{n}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}p(\rho(s,b(s)))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,b(s)))b(s)^{n}{\,\rm d}s\\[2.84526pt]
&\leq C(E_{0},M,T).\end{split}

∎

Remark 3.10
.

In
Step 2
above, when
\kappa=1
, it follows from (
3.47
)
that the boundary term given
by
\kappa\varepsilon a^{n-1}(\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})(t,a)
has a bad sign.
When
\alpha\in(-1,n-2)
, we have

\displaystyle a^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}(t,a)

\displaystyle=\int^{a}_{0}(r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})_{r}{\,\rm d}r=\frac{(n-2)-\alpha}{\omega_{n}}\int_{B_{a}}(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+2)}*\rho)(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}>0.

(3.51)

For
\gamma<\frac{n}{(n-2)-\alpha}
, by the HLS inequality from
Lemma
A.2
and (
3.51
), we have

\displaystyle a^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}(t,a)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(\Omega_{t})}\leq C(E_{0}).

For
\gamma\geq\frac{n}{(n-2)-\alpha}
, using the above and the interpolation, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}a^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}(t,a)&\leq C(M,E_{0}).\end{split}

Thus, with such estimates, in order to control
\kappa\varepsilon a^{n-1}(\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r})(t,a)
,
it suffices to control
\rho(t,a)
.
However, such an a priori estimate on the density at
r=a
has not yet been available.

We now prove the second BD-type estimate result. Here we are able to provide a result
for both the repulsive and attractive case
\kappa\in\{-1,1\}
and for the more singular Riesz potential

\alpha\in(0,n-1)
. However, the estimate given by the Grönwall inequality is rough,
compared to that of the case where integration by parts can be applied, so that
we obtain a different range of
\gamma
for which it can be applied.
This is due to the non-locality of the Riesz potential, while the potential is a local operator
in the already proved Coulomb case
\alpha=n-2
.

Lemma 3.11

(BD-Type Entropy Estimate II)
.

Under the conditions of
Lemma
3.4
, for
\alpha\in(-1,n-1)
,

\gamma\geq\frac{3n}{3n-2(1+\alpha)}
, any given
T>0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,
and
b\geq\max\{C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon),\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\}

(
as defined in
Lemmas
3.8
and
B.8
)
,
the following uniform estimate holds for
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big|(\sqrt{\rho(t,r)})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{4a_{0}\varepsilon}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\big|(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(t,b(t)))b(t)^{n}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{t}p(\rho(s,b(s)))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,b(s)))b(s)^{n}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C(E_{0},M,T).\end{split}

Proof.

We use calculations (
3.40
)–(
3.45
) done in Lemma
3.9
.
Substituting (
3.45
) into (
3.43
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}\,{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x\bigg\}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}

\displaystyle=-\kappa\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})\rho r^{n-1}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{x}{\,\rm d}x.

(3.52)

In the case that
\gamma\geq\frac{3n}{3n-2(1+\alpha)}
, we apply the Hölder inequality
and then the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
to obtain

\displaystyle\int^{b(t)}_{a}|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}(t,r)|^{2}\rho(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{\Omega_{t}}|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t,\boldsymbol{y})|^{2}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{3n}{1+\alpha}}(\Omega_{t})}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{3n}{3n-2(1+\alpha)}}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq C\||\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}*\rho\|^{2}_{L^{\frac{3n}{1+\alpha}}(\Omega_{t})}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{3n}{3n-2(1+\alpha)}}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho\|^{3}_{L^{\frac{3n}{3n-2(1+\alpha)}}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\rho\|^{3\theta}_{L^{\gamma}(\Omega_{t})}\|\rho\|^{3(1-\theta)}_{L^{1}(\Omega_{t})}

\displaystyle\leq C(M,E_{0}),

(3.53)

where
\theta=\frac{2\gamma(1+\alpha)+3n}{3n\gamma}
. Applying the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to (
3.52
)
and using (
3.53
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\tau}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x\bigg\}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x\\
&\quad\,+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\Big)_{\tau}+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\\
&\leq C(M,E_{0})+\int^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}_{0}\frac{1}{2}\big(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x}\big)^{2}{\,\rm d}x.\\
\end{split}

(3.54)

Applying the Grönwall type inequality to (
3.54
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{\tau-s}\bigg\{\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x\Big)_{s}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x+\Big(\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(s)^{n}\Big)_{s}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,\,+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(s)^{n}\bigg\}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C(M,E_{0})\int^{\tau}_{0}e^{\tau-s}{\,\rm d}s+e^{\tau}\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u_{0}+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{0,x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x.\end{split}

Integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\tau}e^{\tau-s}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(s)^{n}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(s)^{n}\Bigg\}{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq e^{\tau}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u_{0}+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{0,x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho_{0})\,{\,\rm d}x\bigg\}+C(M,E_{0})(e^{\tau}-1)+\frac{e^{\tau}}{n}p(\rho_{0}(\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b^{n}.

(3.55)

Using the positivity of the integrands in the integral of the second line in (
3.55
), we conclude the following inequality:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho)\,{\,\rm d}x+\frac{1}{n}p(\rho(\tau,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(\tau)^{n}\\
&\quad\,+\int_{0}^{\tau}\bigg\{\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}p^{\prime}(\rho)\rho_{x}^{2}\,r^{2n-2}{\,\rm d}x+\frac{1}{n\varepsilon}p(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))p^{\prime}(\rho(s,\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b(s)^{n}\bigg\}{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq e^{\tau}\bigg\{\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}\frac{1}{2}(u_{0}+\varepsilon r^{n-1}\rho_{0,x})^{2}{\,\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}}e(\rho_{0})\,{\,\rm d}x\bigg\}+C(M,E_{0})(e^{\tau}-1)+\frac{e^{\tau}}{n}p(\rho_{0}(\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}))b^{n}.\end{split}

∎

3.5.
Higher integrability

In order to apply the compensated compactness framework in Chen-Perepelitsa
[
27
]
to prove
the convergence of weak solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})
of (
1.5
) to
finite-energy solutions of (
1.1
)–(
1.3
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
we need higher integrability uniform estimates of the density and the velocity.
In the following lemma, we require that
\gamma>\frac{1}{n-1-\alpha}
.
Indeed, this is only required so that we can obtain a pointwise estimate
for the derivative of the convolution of the kernel with the density.

Lemma 3.12

(Higher Integrability of the Density)
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the smooth solution of (
2.10
). Then, under the assumptions of
Theorem
2.4
,

T>0
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, and
b\geq\max\{C_{1}(M,E_{0},T,\varepsilon),\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)\}

(
as defined in
Lemmas
3.8
and
B.8
)
,

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{K}\rho^{\gamma+1}(t,r){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\leq C(K,M,E_{0},T)

for any
K\Subset(a,b(t))
and any
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

We follow a similar proof to that of
[
26
]
. Given
K\Subset(a,b(t))
for any
t\in[0,T]
,
there exists
a<d<D
such that
K\Subset(d,D)\Subset(a,b(t))
.
Let
w\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R})
such that
{\rm supp}(w)\subset(d,D)
and
w(r)=1
for all
r\in K
.
Next, we multiply
\eqref{Eul}_{2}
by
w
to obtain the equation:

\displaystyle(\rho uw)_{t}+\big((\rho u^{2}+p(\rho))w\big)_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}\rho u^{2}w+\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}w

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\Big(\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)w\Big)_{r}-\varepsilon\frac{n-1}{r}\rho_{r}uw+\Big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)\Big)\Big)w_{r}.

(3.56)

We integrate (
3.56
) over
[d,r)
and then multiply by
\rho w
to obtain

\displaystyle\rho p(\rho)w^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\rho^{2}\big(u_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u\big)w^{2}-\varepsilon\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}u\rho_{z}w{\,\rm d}z-\Big(\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw{\,\rm d}z\Big)_{t}

\displaystyle\quad-\Big(\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw{\,\rm d}z\Big)_{r}+\rho uw_{r}\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw{\,\rm d}z-\frac{n-1}{r}\rho uw\int_{d}^{r}\rho uw{\,\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\frac{n-1}{z}\rho u^{2}w{\,\rm d}z+\rho w\int_{d}^{r}\Big(\rho u^{2}+p(\rho)-\varepsilon\rho(u_{z}+\frac{n-1}{z}u)\Big)w_{z}{\,\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\rho w\int^{r}_{d}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{z}w{\,\rm d}z=:\sum_{i=1}^{9}I_{i}.

(3.57)

As in
[
26
]
, we have

\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{8}I_{i}\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|\leq C(d,M,E_{0},T).

(3.58)

When

\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)
, employing (
3.1
) and (
3.4
) with the Fubini theorem yields

\displaystyle\left|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}I_{9}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\bigg(\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})}\omega(z,\eta)\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\,\,+\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})^{c}}\omega(z,\eta)\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg){\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\bigg(\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})}\frac{|z-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}}{(r\eta)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\,\,+\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})^{c}}|z-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)}\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg){\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\int_{a}^{b(t)}\big(1+|z-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}\big)\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t.

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T)+C(d,D)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\int^{D}_{d}\rho(t,\eta)|z-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}(\rho w)(t,z){\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}\eta\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t.

(3.59)

For
\gamma>\frac{1}{n-1-\alpha}
, by Theorem
A.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}I_{9}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T)

\displaystyle\quad+C(d,D)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,\eta)\sup_{s\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big\{\int^{D}_{d}|s-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}(\rho w)(t,z){\,\rm d}z\Big\}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle=C(d,M,T)

\displaystyle\quad+C(d,D)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,\eta)\sup_{s\in[d,D]}\Big\{\int^{D}_{d}|s-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}(\rho w)(t,z){\,\rm d}z\Big\}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T)+C(d,D)\|\rho\|_{L^{\gamma}(d,D)}\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,\eta){\,\rm d}\eta\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t.

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

(3.60)

When

\alpha\in(-1,n-2)
, by a similar approach to (
3.59
) and using (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}I_{9}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\bigg(\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})}\omega(z,\eta)\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\quad\,\,+\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})^{c}}\omega(z,\eta)\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg){\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq{\small C(d,M,T)+C\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(z-\frac{d}{2},z+\frac{d}{2})}(r\eta)^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}z\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t}

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T)+C(d)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\int_{a}^{b(t)}\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}z\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T).

(3.61)

When
\alpha=n-2
, using (
3.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}I_{9}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle=\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}(\rho w)(t,r)\Big(\int^{r}_{d}(\rho w)(t,z)\int_{a}^{r}\frac{\omega_{n}}{r^{n-1}}\rho(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}z\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,M,T).

(3.62)

Therefore, integrating (
3.57
) over
[0,T]\times[d,D]
and using (
3.58
)–(
3.62
),
we conclude the desired result.
∎

For the next result, we need to make use of entropy pairs.
As defined in Lax
[
63
]
, a pair of functions
(\eta(\rho,m),q(\rho,m))

is called an entropy pair
of the
1
-D isentropic Euler system if they satisfy

\partial_{t}\eta(\rho(t,r),m(t,r))+\partial_{r}q(\rho(t,r),m(t,r))=0

for any smooth solution
(\rho,m)
of the Euler system. Moreover,
\eta
is called a weak entropy if

\eta|_{\rho=0}=0\qquad\text{ for all fixed $u=\frac{m}{\rho}$}.

From
[
74
]
, it has been shown that any weak entropy pair
(\eta,q)

can be represented by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\eta^{\psi}(\rho,m)=\eta^{\psi}(\rho,\rho u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(\rho;s-u)\psi(s){\,\rm d}s,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle q^{\psi}(\rho,m)=q^{\psi}(\rho,\rho u)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\theta s+(1-\theta)u)\chi(\rho;s-u)\psi(s){\,\rm d}s,\end{cases}

(3.63)

where
\chi(\rho;s-u)=(\rho^{2\theta}-(s-u)^{2})_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}

with
\mathfrak{b}=\frac{3-\gamma}{2(\gamma-1)}
,
\theta=\frac{\gamma-1}{2}
,
and
(\cdot)_{+}=\max\{\cdot\,,0\}
.
Choosing
\psi(s)=\frac{1}{2}s|s|
as in
[
26
]
,
we obtain the corresponding entropy pair
(\eta^{\#}(\rho,m),q^{\#}(\rho,m))
that is
represented by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\eta^{\#}(\rho,m)=\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)=\frac{1}{2}\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\rho^{\theta}s)|u+\rho^{\theta}s|(1-s^{2})_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\,\rm d}s,\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle q^{\#}(\rho,m)=q^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)=\frac{1}{2}\rho\int_{-1}^{1}(u+\theta\rho^{\theta}s)(u+\rho^{\theta}s)|u+\rho^{\theta}s|(1-s^{2})_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\,\rm d}s.\end{cases}

(3.64)

Using (
3.64
), it can be shown that there exists
C_{\gamma}>0
such that the following inequalities hold:

\begin{split}&|\eta^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\gamma}(\rho|u|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}),\quad q^{\#}(\rho,\rho u)\geq C_{\gamma}^{-1}(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}),\\
&|\eta^{\#}_{m}(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\gamma}(|u|+\rho^{\theta}),\quad\quad|\eta^{\#}_{\rho}(\rho,\rho u)|\leq C_{\gamma}(|u|^{2}+\rho^{2\theta}).\end{split}

(3.65)

As in
[
27
,
28
]
, we have

\displaystyle\rho u\partial_{\rho}\eta^{\#}+\rho u^{2}\partial_{m}\eta^{\#}-q^{\#}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta}{2}\rho^{1+\theta}\int_{-1}^{1}(u-\rho^{\theta}s)s|u+\rho^{\theta}s|\,(1-s^{2})_{+}^{\mathfrak{b}}{\,\rm d}s\leq 0.

(3.66)

Lemma 3.13

(Higher Integrability of the Velocity)
.

Let
(\rho,u)
be the smooth solution of (
2.10
). Then,
under the assumption of
Lemma
3.12
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho|u|^{3}+\rho^{\gamma+\theta}\big)(t,r)\,{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)

for any
(d,D)\Subset[a,b(t)]
for any
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

Firstly, multiplying
\eqref{Eul}_{1}
by
r^{n-1}\eta^{\#}_{\rho}
and
\eqref{Eul}_{2}
by
r^{n-1}\eta^{\#}_{m}
, we have

\displaystyle(\eta^{\#}r^{n-1})_{t}+(q^{\#}r^{n-1})_{r}+(n-1)\big(-q^{\#}+\rho u\eta^{\#}_{\rho}+\rho u^{2}\eta_{m}^{\#}\big)r^{n-2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\eta^{\#}_{m}\big((\rho u)_{r}+(n-1)\rho(\frac{u}{r})_{r}\big)r^{n-1}-\kappa\eta^{\#}_{m}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}r^{n-1}.

(3.67)

Integrating (
3.67
) over
[r,b(t))
and using (
2.11
) and (
3.66
), we obtain

\displaystyle q^{\#}(t,r)r^{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq-\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)(\rho u_{z})_{z}\,z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z-(n-1)\varepsilon\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)\left(\frac{u}{z}\right)_{z}\rho\,z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z

\displaystyle\quad\,+\Big(\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}(t,z)\,z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z\Big)_{t}+\big(q^{\#}-u\eta^{\#}\big)(t,b(t))b(t)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\kappa\int_{r}^{b(t)}\eta^{\#}_{m}(t,z)\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{z}z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z=:\sum^{5}_{i=1}I_{i}.

(3.68)

Now, as in
[
26
, Lemma 3.7]
, we have

\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\Big(\sum^{4}_{i=1}I_{i}\Big){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

To estimate the potential term
I_{5}
, we use (
3.65
), the Young inequality, and once again
the estimates in (
3.1
), and apply the same method as for (
3.59
) and (
3.60
)–(
3.61
)
to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\int^{b(t)}_{r}\kappa\eta^{\#}_{m}\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{z}z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\int^{b(t)}_{r}\big((|u|+\rho^{\theta})\rho\big)(t,z)z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\int^{b(t)}_{r}\big(\rho|u|+\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)(t,z)z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{D}_{d}\int^{b(t)}_{r}\big(\rho u^{2}+\rho+\rho^{\gamma}\big)(t,z)z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

(3.69)

Combining
\eqref{1.32}_{2}
with (
3.68
)–(
3.69
), we obtain the desired result.
∎

4.
Global Solutions to the Compressible Navier-Stokes-Riesz Equations

We now show the convergence of our solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})
of the free boundary
problem (
2.10
)–(
2.13
) to global weak solutions
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})
of CNSREs (
1.5
) as
b\to\infty
.
When we take the limit, the cavitation (vacuum states) may form, which should be taken into account carefully.
For the following, we extend
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},u^{\varepsilon,b})
to be zero on
([0,T]\times(0,\infty))\setminus\Omega^{T}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem
2.4
, for fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]

(
as defined in
Lemma
B.8
)
, there exists a non-negative function
\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
a.e. such that, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
)
,

(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}},\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}},\rho^{\varepsilon})\quad\mbox{ {\it a.e.} and strongly in}\ C(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})

(4.1)

for any
q\in[1,\infty)
, where
L^{q}_{\rm loc}
denotes
L^{q}(K)
for
K\Subset(0,\infty)
.

The proof is the same as for
[
26
, Lemma 4.1]
.

Corollary 4.2
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, the pressure function sequence
p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})
is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
for all
q\in[1,\infty]

and, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
)
,

\displaystyle p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\longrightarrow p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{q}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for all $q\in[1,\infty)$}.

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
)
,
the momentum function sequence
m^{\varepsilon,b}:=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}
converges strongly in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
to
some function
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
for all
q\in[1,\infty)
, which particularly implies that

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon,b}=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\qquad\mbox{a.e. in $[0,T]\times(0,\infty)$}.

Proof.

From Lemmas
3.4
,
3.9
, and
3.11
, and the Sobolev embedding theorem,
we see that
\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}
is uniformly bounded
in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{1}_{\rm loc})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{\rm loc})
for
b>0
,
so that

\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)

is uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})
for
b>0
.
Since

\displaystyle\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)_{r}=\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}=2\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\big)_{r}\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)+\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\big),

it follows from Lemmas
3.4
,
3.9
, and
3.11

that
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}
is uniformly
bounded in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}_{\rm loc})
for
b>0
. This implies
that

\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,1}_{\rm loc})\qquad\mbox{uniformly for $b>0$}.

(4.2)

From Lemma
3.4
and the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{r}\big((\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b})^{2}\big)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W_{\rm loc}^{-1,1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{r}\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)^{2}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L_{\rm loc}^{1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\partial_{r}p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\in L^{2}(0,T;H_{\rm loc}^{-1}),\\[5.69054pt]
\displaystyle\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big)_{r}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{\rm loc}),\end{cases}

(4.3)

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}\big)+\frac{n-1}{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}\,\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})

(4.4)

uniformly for
b>0
.
Hence, it follows from (
4.4
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\big)\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}_{\rm loc})

(4.5)

uniformly for
b>0
.
Using Lemmas
3.4
,
3.9
, and
3.11
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\partial_{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}=\frac{2(n-1)}{r}(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}})_{r}\,\big(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}_{\rm loc})

(4.6)

uniformly for
b>0
.
Then, substituting (
4.3
) and (
4.5
)–(
4.6
) into
\eqref{Eul}_{2}

and using the Sobolev embedding theorem, we see that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b})\in L^{2}(0,T;W^{-1,q}_{\rm loc}),

uniformly for
b>0
and
q\in[1,\frac{n}{n-1})
.
Using the Aubin-Lions lemma together with (
4.2
), we obtain that

\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}
is compact in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})
for all
q\in[1,\infty)
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, the limit function
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
in
Lemma
4.3
satisfies that

m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e. on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
. Furthermore, there exists
a function
u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
such that
m^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)
a.e., and
\,u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0
a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}
. Moreover, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
)
,

\displaystyle m^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow m^{\varepsilon}=\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{q}_{\rm loc})$ for $q\in[1,\infty)$},

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}=:\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{\rm loc})$}.

The proof of Lemma
4.4
is the same as
[
26
, Lemma 4.4]
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, let
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
, and let
\zeta(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in C^{1}_{\rm c}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})

be any smooth function with compact support.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{2},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(t_{2},\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(t_{1},\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t.

(4.7)

Moreover, the total mass is conserved:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M\qquad\,\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

(4.8)

The proof of Lemma
4.5
is the same as
[
26
, Lemma 4.9]
.

We can now prove an
a priori
estimate of the energy terms.

Lemma 4.6
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, the following estimates hold
:

(i)

When
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon(n-1)\int^{t}_{0}\int^{\infty}_{0}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}r^{n-3}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\[2.84526pt]
&\leq C(M,E_{0}).\end{split}

(ii)

When
\alpha\in(-1,0]
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}\big(e(\rho^{\varepsilon})+k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\big)\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon(n-1)\int^{t}_{0}\int^{\infty}_{0}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}r^{n-3}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\[2.84526pt]
&\leq C(M,E_{0},T).\end{split}

Proof.

It follows directly from Lemma
3.4
that, for
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{a}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}e(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon(n-1)\int^{t}_{0}\int_{a}^{b(s)}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\Big|^{2}r^{n-3}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\[2.84526pt]
&\leq C(n,M,E_{0},T).\end{split}

Now, applying the Fatou lemma, and Lemmas
3.8
,
4.1
, and
4.4
,
we obtain the result when
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
. The approach is the same when
\alpha\in(-1,0]
.
∎

Notice that our estimates do not involve the potential term;
this is because the convergence of the potential has not yet been proved.
To do this, we require a few more steps.
Since the mass is conserved in the limit, and the local convergence of the density has been verified,
there is no blow-up of mass at the origin or a loss of mass at infinity; this allows us to prove some global convergence results.

Lemma 4.7
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
)
,

\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow\rho^{\varepsilon}\quad\text{ strongly in $C(0,T;L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$ for any $q\in[1,\gamma)$}.

That is, the convergence of
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
is global sub-sequentially in
L^{q}
.

Proof.

For all
\alpha\in(-1,n-1)
, it follows from Lemma
4.1
and (
4.8
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\frac{1}{k}}^{k}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=\frac{M}{\omega_{n}},

(4.9)

as if not, there exists
\varepsilon>0
and
t_{k}\in[0,T]
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{k}}^{k}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{k},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\varepsilon\qquad\mbox{for all $k\in\mathbb{N}$}.

(4.10)

Then there exists a subsequence of
(t_{k})_{k}
given by
(t_{k_{l}})_{l}

and
t_{*}\in[0,T]
such that
t_{k_{l}}\to t_{*}
as
l\to\infty
.
From (
4.8
), we see that, by the monotone convergence theorem, there exists
L\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{L}}^{L}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{*},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r>\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\varepsilon.

Note, for
k_{l}\geq L
, using (
4.10
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{L}}^{L}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{k_{l}},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq\int_{\frac{1}{k_{l}}}^{k_{l}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{k_{l}},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\varepsilon.

(4.11)

By Lemma
4.1
,
\rho^{\varepsilon}\in C(0,T;L^{1}_{\rm loc})
so that

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{L}}^{L}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{k_{l}},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\longrightarrow\int_{\frac{1}{L}}^{L}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{*},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\qquad\mbox{as $k\to\infty$}.

(4.12)

Thus, combining (
4.11
) with (
4.12
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{L}}^{L}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t_{*},r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\varepsilon,

which is a contradiction. Thus, (
4.9
) is verified.

With this, there exists
k=k(\delta)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\frac{1}{k}}^{k}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r>\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\frac{\delta}{4},

(4.13)

which, by (
4.8
) and (
4.13
), implies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\int_{k}^{\infty}+\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{k}}\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\frac{\delta}{4}.

(4.14)

By (
4.1
), there exists
B(\delta)>0
such that, for
b\geq B(\delta)
(up to a sub-sequence),

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\frac{1}{k}}^{k}|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\frac{\delta}{4}.\end{split}

(4.15)

Then it follows from (
4.13
)–(
4.15
) that, for
b\geq B(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\inf_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{\frac{1}{k}}^{k}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r>\frac{M}{\omega_{n}}-\frac{\delta}{2},\\
&\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\int_{k}^{\infty}+\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{k}}\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\frac{\delta}{2}.\end{split}

(4.16)

Thus, we obtain that, for
b>B(\delta)
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}|(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r<\delta.

Now, for
q\in[1,\gamma)
, using Lemmas
3.4
and
4.6
,
and performing interpolation, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|_{C(0,T;L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))}\\
&\leq\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|^{\frac{\gamma-q}{q(\gamma-1)}}_{C(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|^{\frac{\gamma(q-1)}{q(\gamma-1)}}_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))}\\
&\leq C(n,M,E_{0},T,q)\|\rho^{\varepsilon}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|^{\frac{\gamma-q}{q(\gamma-1)}}_{C(0,T;L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))}\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

as
b\to\infty
(up to a sub-sequence).
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, the following statements hold
as
b\rightarrow\infty

(
up to a sub-sequence
):

\displaystyle\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}\quad\mbox{weak-$\ast$ in $L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$ and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

(4.17)

\displaystyle(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})_{r}(t,r)r^{n-1}\quad\mbox{ in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

(4.18)

\displaystyle(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,r)r^{n-1}\quad\mbox{ in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})-\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\longrightarrow 0.

(4.20)

Moreover, when
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,

\displaystyle\|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|(\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\,\mbox{for $t\geq 0$};

(4.21)

when
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\|(\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\leq C(M,E_{0},T,K)\qquad\,\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

(4.22)

Proof.

We divide the proof into five steps.

1. We first see that (
4.17
) and (
4.21
)–(
4.22
) follow
from Corollary
3.6
and the weak-*/weak compactness directly.

2. To show that the weak limit of
\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
, denoted by
\Phi_{\alpha}^{\varepsilon}
,
is indeed
\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}
, we test by a smooth compactly supported
function
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
so that
it can be shown by shifting the convolution from
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
to
\varphi
that, as
b\to\infty
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\varphi){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\varphi){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Using (
4.17
), we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\longrightarrow\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi\,\Phi_{\alpha}^{\varepsilon}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Then, by the fundamental theorem of calculus of variations,
\Phi_{\alpha}^{\varepsilon}=\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}

a.e.
. The result follows.

3. We now prove (
4.18
).
When
\alpha\in(-1,n-1)
,
for
D>0
,
(t,r)\in[0,T]\times[0,D]
, and
b
sufficiently large,
and taking

0<\sigma<{\hat{\sigma}}<\infty
,
we have

\displaystyle\big|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}r^{n-1}-(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})_{r}r^{n-1}\big|(t,r)

\displaystyle=r^{n-1}\Big|\int^{\infty}_{0}\omega(r,\eta)(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq r^{n-1}\Big|\int^{\sigma}_{0}\omega(r,\eta)(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|+r^{n-1}\Big|\int^{\hat{\sigma}}_{\sigma}\omega(r,\eta)(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+r^{n-1}\Big|\int^{\infty}_{\hat{\sigma}}\omega(r,\eta)(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

(4.23)

For
I_{1}
, applying the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq r^{n-1}\Big(\int^{\sigma}_{0}\big((\rho^{\varepsilon,b})^{\gamma}+(\rho^{b})^{\gamma}\big)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big)\Big|\int^{\sigma}_{0}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(M,E_{0})r^{n-1}\Big|\int^{\sigma}_{0}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\Big|^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}.

(4.24)

When
\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)
, using (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle r^{n-1}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\sigma}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg|^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n-1}\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\bigg(\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(\frac{r}{2},\infty)}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta){\,\rm d}\eta+\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(0,\frac{r}{2})}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta){\,\rm d}\eta\bigg)^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n-1}\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\bigg(\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(\frac{r}{2},\infty)}\Big(\frac{|r-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}}{(r\eta)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}\Big)^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}{\,\rm d}\eta+\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(0,\frac{r}{2})}|r-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg)^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq Cr^{n-1}\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\bigg(r^{-(n-1)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(\frac{r}{2},\infty)}|r-\eta|^{(n-2-\alpha)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}{\,\rm d}\eta+r^{1-(\alpha+1)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}\bigg)^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}+Cr^{n-1-\alpha-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq C(D)\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})},

(4.25)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
n-1-\alpha-\frac{1}{\gamma}>0

due to the condition for (
4.18
),
and the second to the last inequality follows from the fact that there exists
C>0
such that,
for small
\sigma>0
,

\int_{(0,\sigma)\cap(\frac{r}{2},\infty)}|r-\eta|^{(n-2-\alpha)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}{\,\rm d}\eta\leq C\qquad\,\mbox{uniformly in $r$},

due to the fact that
(n-2-\alpha)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}>-1
as a consequence of the condition in (
4.18
).

When
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
, applying (
3.1
) and taking the same approach to (
4.25
),
we obtain that, for
\sigma
small enough,

\displaystyle\begin{split}r^{n-1}\left|\int_{0}^{\sigma}\omega^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(r,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\right|^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\leq C(D)\sigma^{n(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\leq C(D)\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}.\end{split}

(4.26)

Thus, combining (
4.24
)–(
4.26
) together, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}I_{1}\leq C(D,M,E_{0})\sigma^{(n-1)(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})}\big(1+\sigma^{1-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\big)\to 0\qquad\text{ as $\sigma\to 0$}.\end{split}

(4.27)

Considering
I_{2}
, for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
, we use (
3.1
) to obtain

\displaystyle I_{2}\leq Cr^{n-1}\int^{\hat{\sigma}}_{\sigma}(r\eta)^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\leq C({\hat{\sigma}})r^{n-2-\alpha}\sigma^{-\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}\int^{\hat{\sigma}}_{\sigma}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(t,\eta){\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\leq C(D,{\hat{\sigma}},\sigma)\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\sigma,{\hat{\sigma}})}.

(4.28)

For
\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)
, using the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
and (
3.4
) yields

\displaystyle I_{2}\leq Cr^{n-1}\int^{\hat{\sigma}}_{\sigma}\frac{|r-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}}{(r\eta)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\leq C({\hat{\sigma}})\Big(\frac{r}{\sigma}\Big)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\big\||\cdot|^{n-2-\alpha}*((\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon})\mathds{1}_{(\sigma,{\hat{\sigma}})})\big\|_{C[0,D]}

\displaystyle\leq C(D,{\hat{\sigma}},\sigma)\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\gamma}(\sigma,{\hat{\sigma}})}.

(4.29)

Combining (
4.28
) with (
4.29
), we obtain via Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}I_{2}\leq C(D,{\hat{\sigma}},\sigma)\sup_{[0,T]}\big(\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\sigma,{\hat{\sigma}})}+\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\gamma}(\sigma,{\hat{\sigma}})}\big)\to 0\qquad\text{as $b\to\infty$}.

(4.30)

Considering
I_{3}
, since
\omega(r,\eta)\leq C|r-\eta|^{-(\alpha+1)}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C|D-{\hat{\sigma}}|^{-(\alpha+1)}\sup_{[0,T]}\int^{\infty}_{\hat{\sigma}}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta

\displaystyle\leq C(M)|D-{\hat{\sigma}}|^{-(\alpha+1)}\to 0\qquad\text{ as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$}.

(4.31)

Thus, combining (
4.23
) and (
4.27
) with (
4.30
)–(
4.31
), we conclude

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}r^{n-1}-(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})_{r}r^{n-1}|(t,r)\to 0\qquad\text{ as $b\to\infty$}.

Thus, (
4.18
) is proved.

4. Regarding (
4.19
), when
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,
using the same method as for the calculations for
\omega

in Lemma
3.1
with (
3.1
) and (
3.3
)–(
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle K(r,\eta)\leq C\min\big\{|r-\eta|^{-\alpha},\,(r\eta)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\}.

(4.32)

That is,
K(r,\eta)
has the same bound as
\omega
from (
3.1
)
but with
\alpha+1
replaced by
\alpha
.
Then the proof of (
4.19
) is verbatim the proof of (
4.18
)
corresponding to the case that
\alpha\in(0,n-2)
.

When
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, taking
{\hat{\sigma}}>1
, we define
J_{\hat{\sigma}}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{1}{{\hat{\sigma}}^{n}}J(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\hat{\sigma}})
,
where
J
is a standard spherically symmetric mollifier kernel
with
J\in C^{\infty}_{\rm c}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

{\rm supp}(J)\subset B_{1}
, and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}J(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=1
.
Using (
2.10
) and integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&=\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\left((\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})(k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2}))_{r}+(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})_{r}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\right)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.\end{split}

Integrating over time, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&=\int^{b}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&\quad+\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\left((\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})(k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2}))_{r}+(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})_{r}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\right)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s.\end{split}

(4.33)

As in (
3.37
), for
\boldsymbol{x}\in K=\overline{B_{D}}
and
q\in(1,\gamma)
,
we use (
3.36
) and (
3.38
) to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})(\boldsymbol{x})-(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(\boldsymbol{x})\big|\\
&\leq\int_{B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x})}k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}+\int_{B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq\|k_{\alpha}(|\cdot|)\|_{L^{\frac{q}{q-1}}(B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}})}\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}(B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x}))}\\
&\quad\,+C\int_{B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}\left(k_{\alpha}(1+D^{2})+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\right)|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}|(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq C({\hat{\sigma}})\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+C(D)\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\
&\quad\,+C\int_{B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\rho^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}+C\int_{B_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}(\boldsymbol{x})^{c}}k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.\end{split}

By Lemma
4.7
, for fixed
{\hat{\sigma}}
, we have

\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}|I_{1}+I_{2}|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$}.

(4.34)

For
I_{3}
, by Lemma
4.6
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
we obtain

\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}|I_{3}|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$, uniformly in $b$}.

For
I_{4}
, by (
4.33
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|I_{4}|&=C\int^{b(t)}_{3{\hat{\sigma}}}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&\leq C\int^{b(t)}_{0}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&=C\int^{b(t)}_{0}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&\quad+C\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{0}\left((\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})(k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2}))_{r}+(\mathds{1}_{B_{2{\hat{\sigma}}}^{c}}*J_{\hat{\sigma}})_{r}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})\right)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C\int^{\beta(t)}_{\hat{\sigma}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{C}{{\hat{\sigma}}^{\alpha+1}}\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{\hat{\sigma}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad+\frac{Ck_{\alpha}(1+{\hat{\sigma}}^{2})}{{\hat{\sigma}}}\int^{t}_{0}\int^{3{\hat{\sigma}}}_{\hat{\sigma}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\leq C\int^{\infty}_{\hat{\sigma}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}k_{\alpha}(1+r^{2})r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\frac{Ck_{\alpha}(1+{\hat{\sigma}}^{2})}{{\hat{\sigma}}}\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&=:I_{4,1}+I_{4,2}.\end{split}

By Lemma
B.4
and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we obtain

\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}|I_{4,1}|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$, uniformly in $b$}.

(4.35)

By Lemma
3.4
and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]\times[0,D]}|I_{4,2}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ck_{\alpha}(1+{\hat{\sigma}}^{2})}{{\hat{\sigma}}}\int^{t}_{0}\int^{b(s)}_{a}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u^{\varepsilon,b})^{2}\big)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(M,E_{0},T)k_{\alpha}(1+{\hat{\sigma}}^{2})}{{\hat{\sigma}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$, uniformly in $b$.}

(4.36)

Thus, combining (
4.34
)–(
4.36
) together yields that, for
\alpha\in(-1,0]
,

(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,r)r^{n-1}\qquad\text{ in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$ as $b\to\infty$}.

5. For (
4.20
), it follows from Lemma
4.7
and (
4.19
)
that, when
\alpha\in(-1,n-1)
and
{\hat{\sigma}}>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\hat{\sigma}}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})-\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$}.

(4.37)

When
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
, using (
4.32
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)\bigg(\Big(\int_{0}^{r-1}+\int_{r+1}^{\infty}\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta+\int_{r-1}^{r+1}\frac{1}{(r\eta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)\bigg(\Big(\int_{0}^{r-1}+\int_{r+1}^{\infty}\Big)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta+\frac{1}{({\hat{\sigma}}-1)^{\alpha}}\int_{r-1}^{r+1}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta\bigg)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

(4.38)

We can also replace
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}
by
\rho^{\varepsilon}
in (
4.38
).
Thus, for
\delta>0
, with (
4.14
), (
4.16
), and (
4.38
),
we obtain that, for
b
and
{\hat{\sigma}}
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})-\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq C(M)\delta.

(4.39)

When
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, using (
3.21
), (
3.23
), and Lemma
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\Big|\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{y})\Big(\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y})}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y})^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\omega_{n}}\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{y})\Big(\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|_{L^{\gamma}(B_{1}(\boldsymbol{y}))}\|k_{\alpha}(|\cdot|)\|_{L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}(B_{1})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\,+C\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\geq 1}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\left(1+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2})\right){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\left(1+k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2})\right){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\\
&\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\Big(\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}-\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|_{L^{1}(B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c})}+\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big).\end{split}

Notice that, by (
4.35
)–(
4.36
),

\sup_{[0,T]}\Big|\int_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$ \,\, uniformly in $b$}.

(4.40)

Thus, for
\delta>0
, by Lemmas
4.6
–
4.7
