, the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, and (
4.40
), we obtain that, for
b
and
{\hat{\sigma}}
sufficiently large,

\int_{\hat{\sigma}}^{\infty}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})-\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\delta.

(4.41)

Therefore, combining (
4.37
) and (
4.39
) with (
4.41
) leads
to (
4.20
) for
\alpha\in(-1,n-1)
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, the following holds
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})\Big)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
3.4
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}e(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}e(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\big(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}\big)\Big)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

Using the construction of the approximation of
(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})
and Lemma
3.8
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}e(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})\Big)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})\Big)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\qquad\text{as }b\to\infty.

Now, by the Fatou lemma, Lemma
3.8
, and (
4.20
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{b\to\infty}\int_{0}^{b(t)}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon,b}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon,b}e(\rho^{\varepsilon,b})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.10
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, let
\boldsymbol{\psi}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\in\left(C^{2}_{0}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})\right)^{n}
be any smooth function with compact support
so that
\boldsymbol{\psi}(T,\boldsymbol{x})=0
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\partial_{t}\boldsymbol{\psi}+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+p(\rho^{\varepsilon})\mbox{\rm div}\boldsymbol{\psi}-\rho^{\varepsilon}\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}{\mathcal{M}}_{0}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\boldsymbol{\psi}(0,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\Big\{\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}\cdot\big(\Delta\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\mbox{\rm div}\,\boldsymbol{\psi}\big)+\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\big(\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}+\nabla\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\cdot\big(\frac{{\mathcal{M}}^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\cdot\nabla\big)\boldsymbol{\psi}\Big\}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{n+1}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}\Big\{V^{\varepsilon}\frac{\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}}{r^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{r}\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big(I_{n\times n}-\frac{\boldsymbol{x}\otimes\boldsymbol{x}}{r^{2}}\big)\Big\}:\nabla\boldsymbol{\psi}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t,

where
V^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))
is a function such that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|V^{\varepsilon}(t,\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t\leq C(E_{0},M)\quad\mbox{for some $C(E_{0},M)>0$ independent of $T>0$}.

The proof of this lemma is the same as the argument for that of
[
26
]
.

Lemma 4.11

(
H_{\rm loc}^{-1}
–Compactness)
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, let
(\eta,q)
be a weak entropy pair defined in (
3.63
)
for any compactly supported smooth function
\psi(s)
in
\mathbb{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{is compact in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

Proof.

We multiply
\eqref{Eul}_{1}
by
\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}:=\eta_{\rho}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})

and
\eqref{Eul}_{2}
by
\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}:=\eta_{m}(\rho^{\varepsilon,b},m^{\varepsilon,b})
.
Then, by the construction of the entropy pair, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})-\kappa\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\Big\{\Big(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b})\Big)_{r}-\frac{n-1}{r}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\Big\}.

(4.42)

Multiplying (
4.42
) by
\phi(t,r)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})
and integrating it
over
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
, and then integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\partial_{t}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}+\partial_{r}q^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\frac{n-1}{r}m^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\eta_{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}+u^{\varepsilon,b}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\big(\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}\big)\phi_{r}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta_{m}^{\varepsilon,b}\frac{n-1}{r}\rho_{r}^{\varepsilon,b}u^{\varepsilon,b}\phi{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\big)_{r}\phi{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t:=\sum^{5}_{i=1}I^{\varepsilon,b}_{i}.

Notice that the terms,
I^{\varepsilon,b}_{i}
for
i=1,\cdots,4
, are the same as in
[
26
, Lemma 4.12]
,
so that they can be handled in the same ways.
However,
I^{\varepsilon,b}_{5}
is different and, as such, requires another approach.

Since
\phi
is compactly supported in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
,
there exist
(d,D)\Subset(0,\infty)
and
T>0

such that
{\rm supp}(\phi)\subset(0,T)\times(d,D)
so that

\displaystyle|I^{\varepsilon,b}_{5}|\leq C\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\Big|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\Big|{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\leq C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int^{b(t)}_{a}\big|\omega(r,\eta)\big|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t.

(4.43)

We need to prove the following estimate:

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\big|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\big|{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\leq C_{\psi}(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

(4.44)

When
\alpha\in(n-2,n-1)
, combining (
4.43
) with (
3.1
)
and using the Fubini theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\left|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\right|{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(r-\frac{d}{2},r+\frac{d}{2})}|\omega(r,\eta)|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(r-\frac{d}{2},r+\frac{d}{2})^{c}}|\omega(r,\eta)|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(r-\frac{d}{2},r+\frac{d}{2})}\frac{|r-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}}{(r\eta)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\quad\,+C_{\psi}(d)\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(r-\frac{d}{2},r+\frac{d}{2})^{c}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(d,M,T)+C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\int_{(a,b(t))\cap(r-\frac{d}{2},r+\frac{d}{2})}\frac{|r-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}}{d^{n-1}}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(d,M,T)+C_{\psi}(d)\int^{T}_{0}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}\int_{d}^{D}|r-\eta|^{n-2-\alpha}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r){\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}t.

(4.45)

Thus, for
\gamma>\frac{1}{(n-1)-\alpha}
, using Theorem
A.1
and (
4.45
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{d}^{D}\big|\eta^{\varepsilon,b}_{m}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b})_{r}\big|{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}\Big\{\int_{d}^{D}|r-z|^{n-2-\alpha}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r){\,\rm d}r\Big\}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle=C_{\psi}\int^{T}_{0}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}\sup_{z\in[d,D]}\Big\{\int_{d}^{D}|r-z|^{n-2-\alpha}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(r){\,\rm d}r\Big\}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(K)\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}\|_{L^{\gamma}(d,D)}\int^{T}_{0}\int^{b(t)}_{a}\rho^{\varepsilon,b}(\eta)\eta^{n-1}{\,\rm d}\eta{\,\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{\psi}(d,D,M,E_{0},T).

When
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
, using the approach to (
4.45
) but instead with the alternate estimate for
\omega
as in (
3.1
) for
\alpha\in(-1,n-2]
,
we obtain (
4.44
).
∎

Thus, to sum up, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 4.12
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma
4.1
, for any
T>0
,
there exists a global spherically symmetric solution
(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})=(\rho^{\varepsilon},\rho^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon})

of CNSREs (
1.5
) in the sense of
Definition
2.2
satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)\geq 0\,\,\,\,a.e.,\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0,\,\,\big(\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\big)(t,r)=\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}(t,r)u^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\quad\,a.e.\,\,\mbox{on $\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)=0\}$},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int^{\infty}_{0}\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=M\qquad\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})-\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\Big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\infty}(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{2})(s,r)\,r^{n-3}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad\leq C(M,E_{0})\qquad\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}e(\rho^{\varepsilon})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})\bigg)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\\
&\quad\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{m^{\varepsilon}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}+\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}e(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})\bigg)(r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\qquad\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|(\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}(t,r)})_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\big((\rho^{\varepsilon}(s,r))^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\big)_{r}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}s\\
&\quad\leq C(M,E_{0},T)\qquad\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{d}^{D}\big(\rho^{\varepsilon}|u^{\varepsilon}|^{3}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+\theta}+(\rho^{\varepsilon})^{\gamma+1}\big)(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t\leq C(d,D,M,E_{0},T)\qquad\mbox{for all $t\in[0,T]$},\end{split}

where
[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
. Let
(\eta,q)
be a weak entropy pair defined in (
3.63
)
for any smooth compact supported function
\psi(s)
on
\mathbb{R}
. Then, for
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})+\partial_{r}q(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\qquad\mbox{is compact in $H^{-1}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}.

As in
[
26
]
, similar to the compensated compactness argument in
[
27
]
,
we can derive that there exists a vector function
(\rho,m)(t,r)
such that

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\qquad\mbox{{\it a.e.} $\,(t,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}$\, as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}$ (up to a sub-sequence)}.

Notice that

m(t,r)=0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)=0\}
.
We can define the limit velocity
u(t,r)
by setting
u(t,r):=\frac{m(t,r)}{\rho(t,r)}

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\,\rho(t,r)\neq 0\}

and
u(t,r):=0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\,:\rho(t,r)=0\,\,\,\mbox{or $r=0$}\}
.
Then

\displaystyle m(t,r)=\rho(t,r)u(t,r).

We can also define
(\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}})(t,r):=\sqrt{\rho(t,r)}u(t,r)
, which is
0

a.e.
on
\{(t,r)\ :\ \rho(t,r)=0\}
.
Moreover, we obtain that, as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}
,

\frac{m^{\varepsilon}}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}}u^{\varepsilon}\longrightarrow\frac{m}{\sqrt{\rho}}\equiv\sqrt{\rho}u\qquad\mbox{strongly in $L^{2}([0,T]\times[d,D],r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r{\,\rm d}t)$}

for any given
T
and
[d,D]\Subset(0,\infty)
, and

\displaystyle(\rho^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon})\longrightarrow(\rho,m)\quad\mbox{in $L^{p_{1}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})\times L^{p_{2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+})$}

(4.46)

for
p_{1}\in[1,\gamma+1)
and
p_{2}\in[1,\frac{3(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+3})
.

Integrating (
4.7
) over
[0,T]
, letting
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
(up to a sub-sequence),
and using (
4.46
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t=\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(0,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t+\int^{T}_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho\zeta_{t}+{\mathcal{M}}\cdot\nabla\zeta\big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}s{\,\rm d}t

(4.47)

for any
\zeta\in C^{1}_{c}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Thus,
in the same way as for Lemma
4.5
, using (
4.47
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})\zeta(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}t=MT.

Then, in the same way as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
, we have

Lemma 4.13
.

Under the assumptions of
Theorem
2.4
, the following statements hold
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0

(
up to a sub-sequence
):

\displaystyle\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}\rm-\!\!\!\rightharpoonup\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho\quad\mbox{weak-$\ast$ in $L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$ and weakly in $L^{2}(0,T;W^{1,\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}^{n}))$},

\displaystyle(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})_{r}(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)_{r}(t,r)r^{n-1}\quad\mbox{ in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

\displaystyle(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})(t,r)r^{n-1}\longrightarrow(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)(t,r)r^{n-1}\quad\mbox{ in $C_{\rm loc}([0,T]\times[0,\infty))$},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\rho^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon})-\rho(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho)\big|(t,r)\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\longrightarrow 0.

In addition, for
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C(M,E_{0})\,\qquad\mbox{for $t\geq 0$};

and, for
\alpha\in(-1,0]
, for any
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho(t)\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho(t)\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\leq C(M,E_{0},T,K)\qquad\,\mbox{for $t\geq 0$}.

Then, similar to
[
26
]
, we can conclude that

(\rho,m)(t,r)
define a global finite-energy
solution of CEREs (
1.1
)
in the sense of Definition
2.1
, which leads to Theorem
2.4
.

5.
Nonlinear Stability of Steady States for the Compressible Euler-Riesz Equations

In this section, we consider the attractive case
(\kappa=1)
,
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
,
and
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
, and
assume that the initial data
(\rho_{0},\mathcal{M}_{0})(\boldsymbol{x})

have finite initial mass and energy.
For
0<M<\infty
, we recall the definition for a set of functions
\varrho
:

\displaystyle X_{M}:=\bigg\{\varrho\in L^{1}_{+}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{\frac{n+\alpha}{n}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\,\,:\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varrho(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M\bigg\}.

For
\varrho\in X_{M},
we recall the definition of the energy functional
\mathcal{G}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\varrho):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big((\varrho e(\varrho))(\boldsymbol{x})+\frac{1}{2}\varrho(\boldsymbol{x})(\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\varrho)(\boldsymbol{x})\Big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

We first focus on
proving the existence, uniqueness, regularity, and other properties of global minimizers of the free energy
\mathcal{G}
on the set
X_{M}
. We include some details for the sake of completeness and clarity of exposition.

We
notice that one can show that the global minimizers
of the functional
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
, if they exist, are actually radially symmetric. This is a very classical result that goes back to
[
4
]
by using symmetric decreasing rearrangement via Lemma
A.4
. The following result on the existence of global minimizers is proven in
[
17
, Theorem 5]
.

Theorem 5.1
.

For
\alpha\in(0,n)
with
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
, there exists a global
minimizer
\tilde{\rho}\in X_{M}
to
\mathcal{G}
.
Moreover, any global minimizer is radially decreasing, compactly supported, and bounded,
which is a stationary solution in the sense of
Definition
2.3
.
Furthermore, any global minimizer satisfies the Euler-Lagrange conditions
:

There exists a constant
(
Lagrangian multiplier
)

\lambda
such that

\begin{cases}(\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))_{\tilde{\rho}}+\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\tilde{\rho}=\lambda&\mbox{ for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\Gamma$},\\[2.84526pt]
(\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})\geq\lambda&\mbox{ for $\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\Gamma$},\end{cases}

where

\Gamma=\{\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,:\,\tilde{\rho}(\boldsymbol{x})>0\}
.

We need some parts of the core of the existence result of global minimizers in order to derive our stability estimates for CEREs. We sketch partially the proof making emphasis only on the relevant elements. The existence part of the proof follows the classical concentration compactness argument introduced by Lions
[
72
]
in the following result:

Theorem 5.2

(
[
72
, Theorem II.1]
)
.

Suppose that

\Psi\in L^{p}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\text{{\rm(}weak $L^{p}$ space{\rm)}}\,\text{ for }p\in(1,\infty),\text{ with }\Psi\geq 0\text{ a.e.},

(5.1)

and

\begin{cases}\Pi:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)\text{ strictly convex,}\\
\lim_{t\to 0^{+}}\Pi(t)t^{-1}=0,\\
\lim_{t\to\infty}\Pi(t)t^{-q}=\infty,\end{cases}

(5.2)

where
q=\frac{p+1}{p}
. Define

\mathcal{A}_{M}:=\bigg\{\varrho\in L^{1}_{+}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\,:\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varrho(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M\bigg\},

and the energy functional as follows
:

\mathcal{F}(\varrho)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\Pi(\varrho(\boldsymbol{x}))-\frac{1}{2}\varrho(\boldsymbol{x})(\Psi\ast\varrho)(\boldsymbol{x})\Big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

(5.3)

Define the minimum of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{A}_{M}
by

F_{M}:=\inf_{\varrho\in\mathcal{A}_{M}}\mathcal{F}(\varrho).

Then, for any minimizing sequence
\{\rho^{j}\}\subset\mathcal{A}_{M}
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{y}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and a minimizer

\tilde{\rho}\in\mathcal{A}_{M}
to
\mathcal{F}

(
i.e.,
\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\rho})=F_{M}
)

with
\{T^{\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\rho^{j}\}
compact in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

T^{\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\rho^{j}\to\tilde{\rho}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
if and only if

F_{M}<F_{m}+F_{M-m}\qquad\text{ for all $m\in(0,M)$}.

(5.4)

We can now use this theorem in our problem since
-\Phi_{\alpha}\in L^{\frac{n}{\alpha}}_{\rm w}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and satisfies (
5.1
).
Thus, we take
p=\frac{n}{\alpha}
,
q=\frac{p+1}{p}=\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
, and the internal energy function
\Pi(\tau):=\tau e(\tau)=\frac{a_{0}}{\gamma-1}\tau^{\gamma}
that satisfies (
5.2
).
So it suffices to prove (
5.4
) for applying Theorem
5.2
.
In order to verify condition (
5.4
), we also employ a corollary from
[
72
]
.

Corollary 5.3

(
[
72
, Corollary II.1]
)
.

Suppose that (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) are satisfied.
If, for some
\beta\in(1,n)
,

\Psi(t\xi)\geq t^{-\beta}\Psi(\xi)\qquad\mbox{for all $t\geq 1$ and $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$ a.e.},

(5.5)

then (
5.4
) is satisfied if and only if
F_{M}<0
.

Denoting by

g_{M}=\inf_{\varrho\in X_{M}}\mathcal{G}(\varrho),

the result in
[
17
, Theorem 5]
shows that
g_{M}<0,\text{ for all }M>0.
This is obtained via a simple scaling argument on a suitably normalized characteristic of a ball by using the homogeneities of the entropy and the interaction parts of functional
\mathcal{G}
to express this as a function of the radius of the ball with competing homogeneities. This shows (
5.4
) for the free energy functional
\mathcal{G}
.

Clearly,
\Phi_{\alpha}
satisfies (
5.5
). In fact, with an equality for
\beta=\alpha
and for all
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\}
that is for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

a.e.
, since
g_{M}<0
, Corollary
5.3
implies that
there exists a minimizer
\tilde{\rho}\in X_{M}
to
\mathcal{G}
over
X_{M}
. The rest of the properties of the global minimizers of
\mathcal{G}
over
X_{M}
are referred to
[
17
]
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

We now prove the existence of a radial minimizer of functional (
5.3
) over
\mathcal{A}_{M}

under conditions (
5.1
) and (
5.2
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Suppose that (
5.1
)–(
5.2
) are satisfied, with
\Psi
radially symmetric and radially decreasing.
Then there exists a radial minimizer
\tilde{\rho}\in\mathcal{A}_{M}
of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{A}_{M}
.
Moreover, any minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{A}_{M}
is the translation of a radially symmetric and decreasing minimizer.

Proof.

Suppose that
\tilde{\rho}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{A}_{M}
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\tilde{\rho}^{*}e(\tilde{\rho}^{*}))(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

where
\tilde{\rho}^{*}
is the symmetric decreasing rearrangement of
\tilde{\rho}
defined in (
A.2
) in Appendix A.
If
\Psi
is spherically symmetric and decreasing in
r
so that
\Psi^{*}=\Psi
, by Lemma
A.4
, we have

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{*}(\boldsymbol{x})(\Psi*\tilde{\rho}^{*})(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}(\boldsymbol{x})(\Psi*\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x},

with the equality only if
\tilde{\rho}=\rho^{*}(\cdot-y)
for some
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Thus,
\mathcal{F}(\rho^{*})\leq\mathcal{F}(\tilde{\rho})
.
Since
\tilde{\rho}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{F}
over
\mathcal{A}_{M}
,
the equality in fact holds,
\tilde{\rho}:=\rho^{*}
is a radial minimizer,
and
\tilde{\rho}
is the translation of a radial minimizer.
∎

We finally discuss further properties of the global minimizers and their uniqueness.
In Carrillo-Hoffmann-Mainini-Volzone
[
17
]
, they proved that,
for the Riesz potential (
\alpha\in[0,n)
) and in the case that
n\geq 1
and
\frac{n+\alpha}{n}<\gamma<\gamma^{*}

with

\gamma^{*}:=\begin{cases}\frac{\alpha-(n-2)}{\alpha-(n-1)}\quad&\text{for }\alpha\in(n-1,n),\\
\infty&\text{for }\alpha\in[0,n-1],\end{cases}

(5.6)

all the global minimizers of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
are steady states of (
1.1
).
Moreover, if
\frac{n+\alpha}{n}<\gamma\leq 2
, then any global minimizers
\varrho\in X_{m}

are in the space
\varrho\in\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.
Furthermore, for
n=1
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
,
the global minimizers of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
and the steady states of (
1.1
) in
X_{M}
are unique (up to translation). Similar results hold for
\alpha=0
as proven in
[
14
]
, so this case is included in (
5.6
).

Calvez-Carrillo-Hoffmann
[
12
]
proved that, for
n\geq 2
,
\alpha\in[n-2,n)
, and
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
,
the steady states of (
1.1
) in
X_{M}
are unique (up to translation).
Thus, for
n\geq 2
,
\alpha\in[n-2,n)
, and
\frac{n+\alpha}{n}<\gamma<\gamma^{*}
,
there is a unique minimizer of
\mathcal{G}
over
X_{M}
(up to translation) that coincides with the unique steady state of (
1.1
) in
X_{M}

(up to translation).
It was also proved that, for the critical case that
\alpha\in[n-2,n)
and
\gamma=\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
,
there exists a unique (up to translation) minimizer of
\mathcal{G}
over
X_{M_{\rm c}}
and a steady state of (
1.1
) in
X_{M_{\rm c}}

for some critical mass
M_{\rm c}
.

Chan-González-Huang-Mainini-Volzone
[
22
]
proved that, for
n\geq 1
,
\alpha\in(0,1)
for
n=1
,
and
\alpha\in[n-2,n)
for
n\geq 2
, and
\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}<\gamma<\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
,
there exist a unique radial steady state
\tilde{\rho}\in X_{m}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to (
1.1
) in
X_{m}
in the sense that

e(\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})=\big(-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})-\mathcal{K}\big)_{+}

(5.7)

is satisfied for
a.e.

\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
\mathcal{K}\geq 0
.
Here, (
5.7
) is derived by dividing the steady form of (
1.1
), in the attractive case (
\kappa=1
),
by the density to obtain

\nabla e(\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})=-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}\nabla\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{x}).

Then, in the case that the density is regular enough, there must exist a constant
\mathcal{K}
such that

e(\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})=-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})-\mathcal{K}=\big(-\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\tilde{\rho})(\boldsymbol{x})-\mathcal{K}\big)_{+}.

Delgadino-Yan-Yao
[
40
]
considered non-local potentials of the form
W*\rho
, where
W:\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{R}
under the following assumptions:

(W1)

W\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\})
is radially symmetric,
W
is an attractive potential and
W^{\prime}(r)>0
for
r>0
, where
r
is the radial variable.

(W2)

W
is no more singular than the locally integrable potential
\Phi_{\alpha}
at the origin, for some
\alpha<n
. That is, there exists some
C>0
such that
W^{\prime}(r)\leq Cr^{-(\alpha+1)}
for
r\in(0,1)
.

(W3)

There exists
C>0
such that
W^{\prime}(r)\leq C
for all
r\geq 1
.

(W4)

Either
W(r)
is bounded for
r\geq 1
or there exists some
C>0
such that, for all
a,b\geq 0
,

W_{+}(a+b)\leq C(1+W(1+a)+W(1+b)),

where
W_{+}:=\max\{W,0\}
.

Under these conditions (W1)–(W4) and
\gamma\geq 2
, there is at most one steady state in
X_{M}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
up to translation. Note that the potential function
\Phi_{\alpha}
, for
\alpha\in[-1,n)
, satisfies conditions (W1)–(W4). Moreover, in
[
18
]
, they proved that, under the same conditions, the steady states with
W(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|)\tilde{\rho}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
are radially decreasing.

Thus, combining
[
17
, Theorem 2]
,
[
12
, Theorem 3]
,
[
22
, Proposition 5.16]
,
[
40
, Theorem 1.1]
, and
[
18
, Theorem 2.2]
, we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 5.5

(Existence and Uniqueness of Steady States and Global Minimizers)
.

(i)

Suppose that
n\geq 1
,
M>0
,
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
, and

(a)

\,\alpha\in(0,1)
for
n=1
,

(b)

\,\alpha\in[n-2,n)
for
n\geq 2
.

Then the steady states of (
1.1
) in
X_{M}
, as defined in
Definition
2.3
,
are unique
(
up to translation
)
and radially decreasing.
Moreover, if
\frac{n+\alpha}{n}<\gamma<\gamma^{*}
, as defined in (
5.6
),
then the global minimizers of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
are steady states and radially decreasing, which implies the uniqueness of the global minimizers in this range.

(ii)

Suppose that
n\geq 2
,
\alpha\in[n-2,n)
, and
\gamma=\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
.
Then there exists a unique minimizer of
\mathcal{G}

that is a steady state of (
1.1
) in
X_{M_{\rm c}}
, which is radially decreasing.

(iii)

Suppose that
n\geq 1
,
M>0
,
\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}<\gamma<\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
, and

(a)

\,\alpha\in(0,1)
for
n=1
,

(b)

\,\alpha\in[n-2,n)
for
n\geq 2
.

Then there exists a unique radially decreasing steady state
\tilde{\rho}\in X_{m}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to (
1.1
)
in
X_{m}
of (
5.7
).

(iv)

Suppose that
n\geq 1
,
M>0
,
\gamma\geq 2
, and
\alpha\in[-1,n-2)
, then there exists a unique radially decreasing steady state
\tilde{\rho}\in X_{m}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to (
1.1
) in
X_{m}
of (
5.7
).

In order to obtain the stability of the unique stationary solution of CEREs in the previous theorem, we need to introduce some new quantities measuring the distance between a general solution and a stationary solution.
Let
\tilde{\rho}
be a minimizer of
\mathcal{G}
on
X_{M}
, and let
\lambda

be the constant in Theorem
5.1
. For
\varrho\in X_{M},
we define the distance:

\displaystyle d(\varrho,\tilde{\rho})

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big((\varrho e(\varrho)-\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))+(\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\tilde{\rho})(\varrho-\tilde{\rho})\big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big((\varrho e(\varrho)-\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))+(\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\tilde{\rho}-\lambda)(\varrho-\tilde{\rho})\big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big((\varrho e(\varrho)-\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))-(\tilde{\rho}e(\tilde{\rho}))_{\tilde{\rho}}(\varrho-\tilde{\rho})\big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x},

where we have used the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varrho(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M
for
\varrho,\tilde{\rho}\in X_{M}
and Theorem
5.1
. Notice that, by convexity of
\varrho e(\varrho)
, then
d(\varrho,\tilde{\rho})\geq 0
for any
\varrho\in X_{M}
.

Let us now connect this to the global finite-energy weak solutions of CEREs obtained
in the previous sections.
Denote
(\rho,\mathcal{M})(t)=(\rho,\mathcal{M})(t,\cdot)
as given in the statement of Theorem
2.6
.
The energy
\mathcal{E}
of the solution to CEREs is given by

\mathcal{E}(\rho(t),\mathcal{M}(t))=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big((\rho e(\rho))(t,\boldsymbol{x})+\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{x})+\frac{1}{2}\rho(t,\boldsymbol{x})(\Phi_{\alpha}\ast\rho)(t,\boldsymbol{x})\Big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

Then, for a given solution
(\rho(t),\mathcal{M}(t))
of CEREs with mass
M
, we have

\mathcal{E}(\rho(t),\mathcal{M}(t))-\mathcal{E}(\tilde{\rho},0)=\mathcal{G}(\rho(t))-\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.

(5.8)

For
\alpha\in(0,n)
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{G}(\rho(t))-\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho})&=d(\rho(t),\tilde{\rho})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho})\,\Phi_{\alpha}*(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.\end{split}

(5.9)

Combining (
5.8
) with (
5.9
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}(\rho(t),\mathcal{M}(t))-\mathcal{E}(\tilde{\rho},0)&=\mathcal{E}(\rho(t),\mathcal{M}(t))-\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho})\\
&=d(\rho(t),\tilde{\rho})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho})\,\Phi_{\alpha}*(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}.\end{split}

(5.10)

Notice that we can estimate the second term in the last equality, which can be obtained by the Hölder inequality and the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
, as such

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho})\,\Phi_{\alpha}*(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\leq C_{n,\alpha}\|\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}.

Remark 5.6
.

Carrillo-Castorina-Volzone
[
14
]
proved that, for
n=2
,
\alpha=0
, and
\gamma>1
,
there exists a unique global minimizer of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
. which is the unique radially decreasing, compactly supported,
and smooth in its support steady state
of (
1.1
) in the sense of distributions
;
however, in
[
14
]
, only the weak compactness of the minimizing sequence was obtained.
In this paper, we require the strong compactness of the minimizing sequence,
allowing us to control the relative potential energy.
For the Riesz potential
(
i.e.,
\alpha\in(0,n)
)
,
we are able to control the relative potential energy by the relative

L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}
–norm of the densities.
However, in the logarithmic potential case, it is no longer apparent to find the natural controlling relative entropy or how we might obtain such a bound, so that
we cannot control the relative potential energy and
use (
5.13
) and (
5.10
) to obtain (
5.14
), since

\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho})\,\Phi_{0}*(\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

does not have a sign. Thus, we are unable to use a contradiction argument as structured in the following argument for this case.

Proof of Theorem
2.6
.

We follow a similar approach as in Rein
[
80
]
, however, we take advantage of having uniqueness of global minimizers modulo translations.
Suppose the statement in Theorem
2.6
is not true, then there exist
\varepsilon>0
and a sequence of global weak solutions
(\rho^{j},\mathcal{M}^{j})
of CEREs associated to the initial data
\rho^{j}_{0}
and a sequence of times
t_{j}>0
such that

\displaystyle d(\rho^{j}_{0},\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho^{j}_{0}-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}^{j}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho^{j}_{0}}}\Big|^{2}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}<\frac{1}{j},

(5.11)

and

\displaystyle d(\rho^{j}(t_{j}),T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho^{j}(t_{j})-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}^{j}}{\sqrt{\rho^{j}}}\Big|^{2}(t_{j})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\geq\varepsilon

(5.12)

for all
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Notice that the sequence of times
t_{j}
can be chosen outside the zero measure set, where the terms in (
5.12
) are well defined, according to Definition
2.1
.

Then, by (
5.11
) and (
5.9
), we have

\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\rho^{j}_{0},\mathcal{M}^{j}_{0})=\mathcal{E}(\tilde{\rho},0)=\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho}).

Since the energy
\mathcal{E}
for the weak solutions is non-increasing from the initial energy by definition, we have

\limsup_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{G}(\rho^{j}(t_{j}))\leq\limsup_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\rho^{j}(t_{j}),\mathcal{M}^{j}(t_{j}))\leq\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\rho^{j}_{0},\mathcal{M}^{j}_{0})=\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho}).

Thus,
\rho^{j}(t_{j})\in X_{M}
is a minimizing sequence of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
and, by Theorem
5.2
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{y}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
T^{\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\rho^{j}(t_{j})\to\tilde{\rho}

in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{\frac{n+\alpha}{n}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, due to the uniqueness of
\tilde{\rho}
up to translation.
Then

\|\rho^{j}(t_{j})-T^{-\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}=\|T^{\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\rho^{j}(t_{j})-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $j\to\infty$},

since
\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}\in(1,\frac{n+\alpha}{n})
.
We see that, for any
i
,

\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathcal{E}(\rho^{j}(t_{j}),\mathcal{M}^{j}(t_{j}))=\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho})=\mathcal{G}(T^{-\boldsymbol{y}_{i}}\tilde{\rho}).

(5.13)

Therefore, by (
5.10
),

d(\rho^{j}(t_{j}),T^{-\boldsymbol{y}_{j}}\tilde{\rho})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}^{j}}{\sqrt{\rho^{j}}}\Big|^{2}(t_{j})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $j\to\infty$}.

(5.14)

This is a contradiction to (
5.12
), which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

Suppose that
\tilde{\rho}
is a unique minimizer of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
. Without loss of generality,
suppose that
0<\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)<\varepsilon
satisfies
Theorem
2.6
. Define

A_{\varepsilon}:=\{\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,:\,\text{\eqref{close}--\eqref{closer} are satisfied with $\varepsilon$ and $\delta(\varepsilon)$ for a global weak solution of CEREs}\}.

Then, for
\boldsymbol{y}\in A_{\varepsilon}
, there exists weak solutions
(\rho,\mathcal{M})
of CEREs such that (
2.8
)–(
2.9
)
are satisfied for
\varepsilon
and
\delta(\varepsilon)
:

\|\tilde{\rho}-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}\leq\|\tilde{\rho}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\|\rho_{0}-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon+\delta(\varepsilon).

Thus, as
\varepsilon\to 0
, we have

\sup_{\boldsymbol{y}\in A_{\varepsilon}}\|\tilde{\rho}-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}\longrightarrow 0.

Since
\tilde{\rho}\in L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we must have

\sup_{\boldsymbol{y}\in A_{\varepsilon}}|\boldsymbol{y}|\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

That is, the closer we start to
\tilde{\rho}
, the closer we remain to
\tilde{\rho}
, not just a translation of
\tilde{\rho}
.

Remark 5.8
.

In case we no longer have the uniqueness of the minimizers, as in
[
80
]
,
we can define the set of minimizers of
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
by

\mathcal{M}_{M}:=\big\{\tilde{\rho}\in X_{M}\,:\,\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\rho})=g_{M}\big\},

and prove the following corollary
:

Corollary 5.9

(Nonlinear Stability of CEREs)
.

Suppose that
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
with
\gamma>\frac{n+\alpha}{n}
.
Let
(\rho,\mathcal{M})(t,\boldsymbol{x})
be a global weak solution of the attractive CEREs
(
\kappa=1
)

in the sense of
Definition
2.1
satisfying

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M.

Then, for any
\varepsilon>0,
there exists
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
such that, if

\displaystyle\inf_{\tilde{\rho}\in\mathcal{M}_{M}}\bigg\{d(\rho_{0},\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho_{0}-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\bigg\}<\delta,

there exists a translation
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle\inf_{\tilde{\rho}\in\mathcal{M}_{M}}\bigg\{d(\rho(t),\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho(t)-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\bigg\}<\varepsilon\qquad\mbox{for a.e. $t>0$}.

Furthermore, let
\tilde{\rho}\in\mathcal{M}_{M}
be an isolated
minimizer
(
up to translation
)
of functional
\mathcal{G}
in
X_{M}
, that is,

\delta_{0}:=\inf\Big\{\|\rho-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}\,:\,\rho\in\mathcal{M}_{M}\setminus\{T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\,:\,\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\}\Big\}>0,

and let

(t,\boldsymbol{y})\longmapsto d(\rho(t),T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho(t)-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}

(5.15)

be continuous. Then, for any
0<\varepsilon<\frac{\delta_{0}}{4}
, there exists
0<\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)<\varepsilon
such that,
if

\displaystyle d(\rho_{0},\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho_{0}-\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big|^{2}\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}<\delta,

there exists a translation
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

\displaystyle d(\rho(t),T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho})+\|\rho(t)-T^{\boldsymbol{y}}\tilde{\rho}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\frac{\mathcal{M}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\Big|^{2}(t)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}<\varepsilon\qquad\mbox{for all $t>0$}.

Notice that this result needs the additional continuity assumption of (
5.15
)
;
see
[
80
]
for details.

Appendix A
Convolution Inequalities

We now present several convolution inequalities that have been used in the main text of this paper.

Theorem A.1

(
[
43
, Theorem II.11.2]
)
.

Suppose that
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
is bounded,
\alpha\in(0,n)
, and
f\in L^{q}(\Omega)
for
q\in(1,\infty)
.
If
\alpha<n(1-\frac{1}{q})
,
then
|\cdot|^{-\alpha}*(\mathds{1}_{\Omega}f)\in C^{0,\mu}(\overline{\Omega})

for
\mu:=\min\big\{1,n(1-\frac{1}{q})-\alpha\big\}

and there exists some constant
C=C(\Omega,n,q,\alpha)
such that

\big\||\cdot|^{-\alpha}*(\mathds{1}_{\Omega}f)\big\|_{C^{0,\mu}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}.

Lemma A.2

(Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev (HLS) Inequality)
.

Let
\alpha\in(0,n)
and
1<p,r<\infty
such that

\frac{1}{p}+\frac{\alpha}{n}=1+\frac{1}{r}.

Then,
for all
f\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\big\||\cdot|^{-\alpha}*f\big\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C_{n,\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

where

C_{n,\alpha}=\pi^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\frac{\Gamma(\frac{n-\alpha}{2})}{\Gamma(n-\frac{\alpha}{2})}\bigg(\frac{\Gamma(\frac{n}{2})}{\Gamma(n)}\bigg)^{\frac{\alpha-n}{n}}.

Lemma A.3

(Variation of HLS Inequality
[
11
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

Suppose that
\alpha\in(0,n)
and
p\geq\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}
.
Then there exists
C_{*}=C_{*}(\alpha,p,n)>0
such that, for any
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(\boldsymbol{x})|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{-\alpha}f(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\bigg|\leq C_{*}\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{p(2n-\alpha)-2n}{n(p-1)}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}^{\frac{\alpha p}{n(p-1)}}.

(A.1)

In particular, when
p=\frac{n+\alpha}{n}>\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}
, for any

f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\cap L^{\frac{n+\alpha}{n}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(\boldsymbol{x})|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{-\alpha}f(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\bigg|\leq C_{*}\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{\frac{n-\alpha}{n}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{n+\alpha}{n}}}^{\frac{n+\alpha}{n}}.

Furthermore,
C_{*}
is the sharp constant that satisfies (
A.1
) and is strictly bounded above by the standard HLS constant
C_{n,\alpha}
, i.e.,
C_{*}<C_{n,\alpha}
.

Lemma A.4

(Riesz’s Rearrangement Inequality
[
68
]
)
.

Let
f,g,h:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be measurable functions. Define

I(f,g,h):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(\boldsymbol{x})g(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})h(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}.

For a given measurable function
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
, define the symmetric decreasing rearrangement
\phi^{*}
of
\phi
is

\phi^{*}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\int^{\infty}_{0}\mathds{1}_{\{\boldsymbol{y}\,:\,\phi(\boldsymbol{y})>t\}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}t

(A.2)

with

A^{*}=\{\boldsymbol{x}\,:\,\omega_{n}|\boldsymbol{x}|^{n}\leq|A|\}

for any Lebesgue measurable set
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

I(f,g,h)\leq I(f^{*},g^{*},h^{*}).

(A.3)

If
g
is a strictly symmetric-decreasing function, then the equality holds in (
A.3
)
if and only if there exists some
\boldsymbol{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
f(\boldsymbol{x})=f^{*}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{z})
and
h(\boldsymbol{x})=h^{*}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{z})
.

Theorem A.5

(The Riesz Composition Formula
[
79
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

Suppose that
0<\alpha<n
,
0<\beta<n
, and
0<\alpha+\beta<n
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{z}|^{\alpha-n}|\boldsymbol{z}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{\beta-n}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{z}=\kappa_{\alpha,\beta}\,|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{\alpha+\beta-n},

where

\kappa_{\alpha,\beta}=\frac{\pi^{\frac{n}{2}}\Gamma(\frac{\alpha}{2})\Gamma(\frac{\beta}{2})\Gamma(\frac{n-\alpha-\beta}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{n-\alpha}{2})\Gamma(\frac{n-\beta}{2})\Gamma(\frac{\alpha+\beta}{2})}.

(A.4)

Appendix B
Construction of the Approximate Initial Data

Similar to
[
26
]
, we approximate the initial density by a density function away from the vacuum,
which means that our approximate solutions can also be away from the vacuum, giving us simpler calculations,
as we consider the equations in terms of the velocity and the density, rather than the momentum and the density.

Consider a standard radially symmetric mollifier
J(\boldsymbol{x})
and the associated mollifier:

J_{\delta}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{1}{\delta^{n}}J(\frac{\boldsymbol{x}}{\delta}).

We now define the approximation:

\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})}J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}+\varepsilon e^{-|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}}\bigg)^{2}.

We have defined the approximation as such in order to have a nice formula for
\sqrt{\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}}(\boldsymbol{x})
with the first part smoothing the square root of the density and the second part avoiding any vacuum states that might occur in the initial density.
Then, as in
[
26
]
, we normalize to ensure that the mass of the approximating initial density remains constant as
\varepsilon
varies.
We define the initial density approximation:

\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}).

(B.1)

Then, similar to
[
26
]
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma B.1
.

Let
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
for
\kappa=1
and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}\}
for
\kappa=-1
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M,\quad\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}}\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+1\big)\qquad\,\,\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$},\\
&\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\big(\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}}+\|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\|_{L^{2q}}\big)=0,\\
&\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})}\Big|^{2}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\varepsilon\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big)\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0$}.\end{split}

(B.2)

In the case that
\alpha\in(-1,0]
,

\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}
is well-defined:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(|\sqrt{\rho_{0}}*J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}|^{2}(\boldsymbol{y})+\varepsilon^{2}e^{-2|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}}\Big)|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigg(\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}}(\boldsymbol{y}-\boldsymbol{z})J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\boldsymbol{z}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{z}\Big|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}e^{-2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}}\bigg)|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}}\bigg(\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\boldsymbol{z})\sqrt{(|k_{\alpha}(|\cdot|)|*\rho_{0})(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{z})}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{z}\Big)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}}\,|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\bigg)

\displaystyle<\infty,

(B.3)

where we have used the Minkowski inequality to obtain the penultimate inequality.
We can also prove the following convergence result for the kernel:

Lemma B.2
.

\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}
with
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
satisfies the following
:

\begin{split}&\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}}\leq C\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0,\\[5.69054pt]
&\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}}\leq C\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0.\end{split}

For
\alpha\in(-1,0]
and
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}(K)}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0.

(B.4)

Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C.

(B.5)

Proof.

The first part is an application of the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
. For simplicity, we may assume that
K=B_{D}
for some
D>0
.
Then, for
{\hat{\sigma}}>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\\
&\leq\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\\
&=:I_{1}+I_{2}.\end{split}

Since
\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}}\Phi_{\alpha}\in L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}
,
then, for
I_{1}
, by the Hölder inequality,

I_{1}\leq\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\gamma}}\|\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}}\Phi_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\frac{\gamma}{\gamma-1}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0.

(B.6)

We now want to show that
\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}\to 0
as
{\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty
.
We take
{\hat{\sigma}}
large enough so that, for all
\boldsymbol{x}\in B_{D+1}
and
\boldsymbol{y}\in B_{{\hat{\sigma}}-D-1}^{c}
,

1\leq|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|\leq\min\{2|\boldsymbol{y}|,|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}\}
.
Then, for any
\boldsymbol{x}\in B_{D+1}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}\big|(\boldsymbol{x})\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{y})\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq\int_{B_{{\hat{\sigma}}-D-1}^{c}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{y})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\\
&\leq C\int_{B_{{\hat{\sigma}}-D-1}^{c}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{y})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}.\end{split}

This implies

\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}\leq C\int_{B_{{\hat{\sigma}}-D-1}^{c}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{y})|k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{y}|)|{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$}.

By the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\to M\qquad\text{ as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Then, for
\varepsilon
small enough,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\geq\frac{M}{2}
.
Moreover, for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
small enough, if
\boldsymbol{x}\in K
, by a similar calculation to (
B.3
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big|(\boldsymbol{x})\\
&\leq\frac{2M}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}}\bigg(\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}J_{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\boldsymbol{z})\sqrt{((\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0})(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{z})}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{z}\Big)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varepsilon^{2}e^{-2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}}(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y}\bigg)\\
&\leq 4\Big(\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}+\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*e^{-2|\,\cdot\,|^{2}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}\Big).\end{split}

This yields

I_{2}\leq 5\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*\rho_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}+4\big\|(\mathds{1}_{B_{\hat{\sigma}}^{c}}|\Phi_{\alpha}|)*e^{-2|\cdot|^{2}}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{D+1})}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as ${\hat{\sigma}}\to\infty$}.

(B.7)

Thus, combining (
B.6
) with (
B.7
), we obtain

\big\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

For the final part, take
1<p<\min\{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2},\gamma\}
, by applying the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
, the interpolation argument,
and (
3.7
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\\
&\leq\big\|(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}\mathds{1}_{B_{1}})*(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}+\big\|(|\cdot|^{-(\alpha+1)}\mathds{1}_{B_{1}^{c}})*(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\\
&\leq\big\|(|\cdot|^{-(1+\alpha/2+n(1-1/p))})*(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}+\big\|\mathds{1}_{B_{1}^{c}}*(\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\\
&\leq C_{n,1+\alpha/2+n(1-1/p)}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|K|^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2n}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\\
&\leq C_{n,1+\alpha/2+n(1-1/p)}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma-p}{p(\gamma-1)}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{\gamma(p-1)}{p(\gamma-1)}}+|K|^{\frac{\alpha+2}{2n}}\|\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.\end{split}

Thus, we conclude

\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}(K)}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Now, by using the initial assumption (
2.3
), there exists
C>0
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C.

Thus, applying the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}+\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\big)k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\\
&\leq C+\Big|\int_{B_{1}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\big)k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|+\Big|\int_{B_{1}^{c}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\big)k_{\alpha}(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\\
&\leq C+k_{\alpha}(2)\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(B_{1})}+\Big|\int_{B_{1}^{c}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\big)k_{\alpha}(2|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\\
&\leq C+k_{\alpha}(2)\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(B_{1})}+k_{\alpha}(2)\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(B_{1}^{c})}+C\Big|\int_{B_{1}^{c}}\big(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})\big)k_{\alpha}(|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\Big|\\
&=C\big(1+\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|\Phi_{\alpha}*(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0})|(\boldsymbol{0})\big).\end{split}

Now, by (
B.2
) and (
B.4
), we have

\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+|\Phi_{\alpha}*(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}-\rho_{0})|(\boldsymbol{0})\longrightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

Then there exists some
C>0
such that, for small enough
\varepsilon
, (
B.5
) holds.
∎

As in the previous literature, in order to prove the BD entropy and the convergence of
\Omega^{T}
to
[0,T]\times(0,\infty)

in some sense,
we require that

\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(b)\cong b^{-(n-\beta)},

(B.8)

where
\beta=\min\{\frac{1}{2},(1-\frac{1}{\gamma})n\}
.
To do this, we choose a cut-off function
S\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, such that

\begin{cases}S(z)=0\quad\text{ if }z\in(-\infty,0],\\
S(z)=1\quad\text{ if }z\in[1,\infty),\\
S(z)\text{ is monotonic in }[0,1].\\
\end{cases}

Now, as in
[
26
]
, we define

\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\Big\{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})}\big(1-S(2(|\boldsymbol{x}|-(b-1)))\big)+b^{-\frac{n-\beta}{2}}S(2(|\boldsymbol{x}|-(b-1)))\Big\}^{2}.

Then
\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
is a radial function and satisfies
\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(b)=b^{-(n-\beta)}
.
Now, as before, we normalize
\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
(but on the corresponding domain from problem (
2.10
)–(
2.13
): the annulus
\{b^{-1}\leq|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b\}
) to produce

\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{M}{\int_{b^{-1}\leq|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b}\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}}\mathds{1}_{\{b^{-1}\leq|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b\}}(\boldsymbol{x})\tilde{\rho}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}).

(B.9)

As before, it is direct to see that
(
B.8
)
is satisfied.
Similar to
[
26
]
, one can show the following lemma:

Lemma B.3
.

The defined function
\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})
in (
B.9
) is smooth.
Moreover, for any
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
when
\kappa=1
and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}\}
when
\kappa=-1
,
the following estimates hold
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{b^{-1}\leq|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=M\qquad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$ and $b>1$},\\
&\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b}\Big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})-\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})\big|^{q}+\Big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})}\Big|^{2q}\Big)\,{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as $b\to\infty$},\\
&\varepsilon\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b}\Big|\nabla_{\boldsymbol{x}}\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})}\Big|^{2}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C\big(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}}+1\big).\end{split}

We also have the convergence of the initial potentials, represented by the following lemma:

Lemma B.4
.

\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
with
\alpha\in(0,n-1)
satisfy

\begin{split}&\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha}}}\leq C\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $b\to\infty$},\\
&\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}}\leq C\|\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $b\to\infty$}.\end{split}

For
\alpha\in(-1,0]
and
K\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\|\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(K)}+\|\nabla(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}-\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})\|_{L^{\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}}(K)}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $b\to\infty$},

Moreover, there exists a constant
C>0
such that, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small and
b
sufficiently large,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\log(1+|\boldsymbol{x}|^{2}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}\leq C.

(B.10)

Proof.

The first part is an application of the HLS inequality from Lemma
A.2
. The second part follows the same approach as for Lemma
B.2
.
∎

We now provide an approximation to the initial velocity. To avoid issues regarding the vacuum, we construct
u^{\varepsilon}_{0}
like so

u^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})}}\Big(\frac{m_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{x}).

(B.11)

As in
[
26
, Lemma A.8]
, we can prove the following:

Lemma B.5
.

As defined above,
u^{\varepsilon}_{0}
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})|u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|m_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})|^{2}}{\rho_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x},

\displaystyle\,\,\,\big\|\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}u^{\varepsilon}_{0}-m_{0}\big\|_{L^{1}}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{ as $\varepsilon\to 0$}.

We can now define
\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
:

\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big(\frac{m_{0}\mathds{1}_{[4b^{-1},b-2]}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{x}-\boldsymbol{y})J_{b^{-1}}(\boldsymbol{y}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{y},

where we have used the indicator function
\mathds{1}_{[4b^{-1},\,b-2]}
so that
{\rm supp}(\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})\subset[2b^{-1},\,b-1]
,
which implies that the lower boundary condition is satisfied.
We can now add the stress-free boundary condition to the upper boundary.
In order to satisfy (
2.12
), we construct an initial velocity that satisfies

\Big(p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})-\varepsilon\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0,r}+\frac{n-1}{r}u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})\Big)(r)=0\qquad\mbox{for $r\in(b-\frac{1}{4},b]$}.

Given a solution to the above, then

\displaystyle(u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}r^{n-1})_{r}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\frac{p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(r))}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(r)}r^{n-1}\qquad\mbox{for $r\in(b-\frac{1}{4},b)$.}

Integrating over
(r,b)
and choosing
u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(b)=0
, we obtain

u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(r)=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\int^{b}_{r}\frac{p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(z))}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(z)}z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z.

Hence, in order to add this boundary condition to
\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
, we use the construction from
[
26
]
:

u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x}):=\tilde{u}^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(\boldsymbol{x})-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}S(4(|\boldsymbol{x}|-(b-\frac{1}{2})))\frac{1}{|\boldsymbol{x}|^{n-1}}\int^{b}_{|\boldsymbol{x}|}\frac{p(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(z))}{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(z)}z^{n-1}{\,\rm d}z.

(B.12)

Then
u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
satisfies (
2.12
). As in
[
26
, Lemma A.9]
, we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma B.6
.

For given
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{b\rightarrow\infty}\int_{|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b}\Big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\Big|^{2}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\Big|\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})\Big|^{2}{\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\boldsymbol{x})\longrightarrow\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})}u_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{x})\qquad\mbox{in $L^{2}(\{|\boldsymbol{x}|\leq b\})\,$ as $b\rightarrow\infty$}.

We define the approximate initial energy as follows:

Definition B.7
.

Define the energy for the approximate initial data by

\displaystyle E_{0}^{\varepsilon}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\bigg|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}}}\bigg|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon})\bigg)(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}<\infty,

and

\displaystyle E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\bigg|\frac{{\mathcal{M}}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}}}\bigg|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}e(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})\bigg)(\boldsymbol{x}){\,\rm d}\boldsymbol{x}<\infty.

Combining all together, we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma B.8
.

Take
\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0},\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon}_{0}
, and
u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}
to be defined as in (
B.1
), (
B.9
),
(
B.11
), and (
B.12
) respectively.
Then
(\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0},u^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})\in C^{\infty}([b^{-1},b])
and satisfies (
2.12
).
For
q\in\{1,\gamma\}
when
\kappa=1
and
q\in\{1,\gamma,\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha}\}
when
\kappa=-1
, the following statements hold
:

(a)

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=M,\qquad\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0}(r)}|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq C\varepsilon(M+1).

As
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E^{\varepsilon}_{0}\longrightarrow E_{0},\\
&(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m_{0}^{\varepsilon})(r)\longrightarrow(\rho_{0},m_{0})(r)\quad\mbox{in $L^{q}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)\times L^{1}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)$},\\
&(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})_{r}\to(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho_{0})_{r}\quad\mbox{in $L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)$}.\end{split}

Then there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that

M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,\alpha}(\gamma)\qquad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$ and $\gamma\in(\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha},\frac{n+\alpha}{n}]$}.

(b)

For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,

\int_{b^{-1}}^{b}\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}(r)\,\omega_{n}r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r=M,\qquad\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{b}\big|\partial_{r}\sqrt{\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0}(r)}\big|^{2}\,r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r\leq C\varepsilon(M+1).

Moreover, for any fixed
\varepsilon>0
, as
b\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{0}^{\varepsilon,b}\longrightarrow E_{0}^{\varepsilon},\\
&(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon,b},m_{0}^{\varepsilon,b})(r)\longrightarrow(\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon},m^{\varepsilon}_{0})(r)\qquad\mbox{\rm in $L^{q}([0,b];r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)\times L^{1}([0,b];r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)$},\\
&(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon,b}_{0})_{r}\to(\Phi_{\alpha}*\rho^{\varepsilon}_{0})_{r}\qquad\mbox{\rm in $L^{\frac{2n}{\alpha+2}}([0,\infty);r^{n-1}{\,\rm d}r)$}.\end{split}

Then there exists
\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)>0
such that

M<M_{\rm c}^{\varepsilon,b,\alpha}(\gamma)\qquad\mbox{for all $\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]$, $b\geq\mathfrak{B}(\varepsilon)$, and $\gamma\in(\frac{2n}{2n-\alpha},\frac{n+\alpha}{n}]$}.
